        "claim": "_variants_md builds the recommendation line with `gap = f\"（高 {abs(pr - po):.1f} 个百分点）\"`. The engine picks the recommended version by combined score (`max(variants, key=score)`, where score is pct minus rate/pitch penalties), not by pct. The recommended version can therefore have the LOWER percentage, and the UI then falsely says it is higher. This breaks R5f's honest-wording requirement and the R5e line \"更像你的原声（相似度高 …）\".",
        "scenario": "Ran `_variants_md` with A {pct 98.2, score 0.95, recommended} and B {pct 99.1, score 0.93}. Output: \"⭐ 推荐：版本 A，更像你的原声（高 0.9 个百分点）\", while the cards right above show A 98.2% and B 99.1%.",
        "fix": "Only say \"高 X 个百分点\" when pr > po. Otherwise explain that A is recommended on the combined score (语速/音高更接近), e.g. \"百分比低 0.9，但综合得分高 0.020\".",
        "lens": "requirements",
        "votes": "3/3",
        "confirmed": true,
        "evidence": "I could not refute this one. It is a real defect, though minor.\n\n1) How the engine picks: voicetwin/synth/engine.py:944 chooses the recommended version with `max(variants, key=lambda v: (v[\"score\"], ...))`. `score` comes from `_version_score`. It is the duration-weighted mean of Scorer.score().total, which is pct/100 minus 0.6*rate_dev, minus 0.3*pitch_dev, minus clip and other penalties (voicetwin/eval/metrics.py:303-380). The per-version `pct` is a separate weighted mean of pct alone. The two can rank A and B differently.\n\n2) The flip can happen in practice. Version B is the denoised copy of the same audio, so the two pct values are usually very close (pct is also capped at 100). Denoising lowers the noise floor. That changes auto_silence_threshold and therefore the voiced duration in speech_activity (voicetwin/utils/audio.py:182-197), which changes rate_dev, and f0 stats can shift too. A voiced-time change of about 2% moves the rate penalty by about 0.012. That outweighs a pct gap of 0.9 points (0.009). So the version with the lower pct winning on score is a normal outcome.\n\n3) What the UI says: voicetwin/webui/app.py:1131-1135 computes `gap = f\"（高 {abs(pr - po):.1f} 个百分点）\"` whenever |pr-po| >= 0.05. It never checks that pr > po. The engine's own log line (engine.py:950-952) uses a signed gap, but the UI uses abs() and the word 高.\n\n4) Reproduced with the py3.9 venv (PYTHONPATH=repo, script at scratchpad/review/vmd_check.py), using A{pct 98.2, score 0.95, recommended} and B{pct 99.1, score 0.93}. The output has the cards \"版本 A：未去杂音（像你本人 98.2%）⭐ 推荐\" and \"版本 B：去杂音（像你本人 99.1%）\", followed by \"⭐ 推荐：版本 A，更像你的原声（高 0.9 个百分点）\". That last line is false next to the numbers shown right above it.\n\n5) No other code handles this case. The existing tests cover only rec pct > other (test_webui_helpers.py:308) and equal pcts (test_integration.py:288). R5f explicitly requires honest wording, and R5e asks for the recommendation line to report the gap on the metric used to rank. The impact is cosmetic but visible to the user, and it contradicts the cards on the same screen. The fix is a one-line condition: only say 高 X 个百分点 when pr > po, otherwise mention the combined score.\n---\nI tried to refute this and couldn't. The defect is real and reachable.\n\n1) Selection and display use different metrics. In voicetwin/synth/engine.py, `_make_variants` (around lines 941-947) picks the recommended version with `best = max(variants, key=lambda v: (v[\"score\"], ...))`. In `_version_score`, `score` is the duration-weighted mean of `Scorer.score().total`. In voicetwin/eval/metrics.py that total is `pct/100 - 0.6*rate_dev - 0.3*pitch_dev - ...` (clip and pause penalties too). The displayed `pct` is a separate duration-weighted mean, rounded to 0.1. Nothing makes the version with the higher score also have the higher pct.\n\n2) The UI hides the sign. In voicetwin/webui/app.py, `_variants_md` (lines 1133-1135) does `if abs(pr - po) >= 0.05: gap = f\"（高 {abs(pr - po):.1f} 个百分点）\"` and never checks that pr > po. The engine's own note line uses a signed gap plus the combined scores, which shows the author expected the two orderings could disagree. The UI line doesn't handle that case.\n\n3) I ran `_variants_md` on HEAD in the gsv39 venv (scratchpad/review/s2_variants.py) with A {pct 98.2, score 0.95, recommended} and B {pct 99.1, score 0.93}. It printed \"版本 A …（像你本人 98.2%）⭐\", then \"版本 B …（像你本人 99.1%）\", then \"⭐ 推荐：版本 A，更像你的原声（高 0.9 个百分点）\". That last line is false.\n\n4) Is this only theoretical? I checked with scratchpad/review/s2_pen.py, using the real `denoise_light` and `Scorer` on synthetic voiced clips at three noise levels. Denoising changes the non-pct part of each sentence's total (pitch_dev, and sometimes rate) by about ±0.001-0.004, in either direction. That equals ±0.1-0.4 percentage points. Denoising generated speech usually moves A-vs-B pct only a little, and pct is rounded to 0.1. Near the 99-100% range the user is aiming for, the 100% cap shrinks the gap further. So a version with the higher score but a lower (or rounded-lower) pct is plausible in real perfect-tier runs. Then the UI says \"高 0.1 个百分点\" while the cards right above show the opposite.\n\nThis breaks R5f's \"Implement HONESTLY\" / honest wording requirement. It also contradicts R5e's intended line \"（相似度高 …）\", which is supposed to be true. Severity is minor and the fix is trivial: only say \"高\" when pr > po, and otherwise explain that the recommendation comes from the combined score.\n---\nI could not refute this. I confirmed it by reading the code and running it.\n\n1) How the engine picks the recommended version (<仓库>/voicetwin/synth/engine.py:943). It uses `best = max(variants, key=lambda v: (v[\"score\"], ...))`. That `score` is the duration-weighted mean of `Scorer.score().total` (engine.py:894-914). Per voicetwin/eval/metrics.py:316-358, `total` = pct/100 − 0.6·rate_dev − 0.3·pitch_dev − other penalties, which is not pct alone. pct is averaged separately and rounded to 0.1. So the recommended version can have the lower percentage. This is a realistic case, not just a theoretical one:\n- Version B is the same audio after noisereduce.\n- `speech_activity` (voicetwin/utils/audio.py:182) uses `auto_silence_threshold`, which depends on the noise floor. Denoising therefore changes the measured voiced duration, so rate_dev changes. f0_stats also shifts.\n- A 1% change in voiced duration moves the score by about 0.006, the same size as a 0.6 percentage-point pct difference.\n\n2) How the UI words it (voicetwin/webui/app.py:1133-1135). It uses `abs(pr - po)` and never checks the sign. The next branch (the \"百分比差不多，综合得分高\" case for equal pct) shows the author meant the pct line only for when the recommended version really has the higher %. They just never handled the reversed case.\n\n3) Experiment: scratchpad/review/sk3_variants.py, run in the gsv39 venv with PYTHONPATH set to the repo. Input: A {pct 98.2, score 0.95, recommended} and B {pct 99.1, score 0.93}. Output:\n\"1. 版本 A：未去杂音（像你本人 98.2%）⭐ 推荐 / 2. 版本 B：去杂音（像你本人 99.1%） / ⭐ 推荐：版本 A，更像你的原声（高 0.9 个百分点）\"\nThe recommendation line says A is higher, contradicting the two cards directly above it.\n\n4) Nothing upstream fixes this. Variants go unchanged from engine `_make_variants` through NarrationResult to `_variants_md`. `_PCT_KEYS` includes \"pct\", so the percentage path is always taken in the perfect tier.\n\nThis is a factually false statement shown to the user. It breaks R5f's honest-wording requirement and R5e's \"report the numbers\". It is minor but real for this release, and the fix is small: only say \"高 X 个百分点\" when pr > po, and otherwise say the recommendation comes from the combined score (语速/音高)."

The other tiers read early_pct instead. Nothing else in voicetwin reads similarity.target_pct: grep finds only engine.py and default_config.yaml, and the web UI and the .md docs never mention it. So the line `similarity: target_pct: 99  # 「完美」档的目标：达到就提前停止` at default_config.yaml:169 is a documented knob that does nothing.\n\nExperiment: I ran Narrator in the py3.9/gradio-4.24 venv (PYTHONPATH=repo, tier='high', quality='perfect', stub project and backend; script at <草稿目录>/review/tp3.py).\n- cfg {\"similarity\":{\"target_pct\":95}} gives target_pct 99.0, so the key is ignored.\n- Adding {\"synth\":{\"tiers\":{\"perfect\":{\"target_pct\":95}}}} gives 95.0.\n\nBoth target_pct and this config key are new in this release; neither appears in b518011.\n\nWhy it is only minor:\n- The default value (99) equals the preset, so default behaviour is correct.\n- A working way to change the target exists (synth.tiers.perfect.target_pct), and the commented example at default_config.yaml:153 shows it.\n- target_pct only takes effect when sim_filter is on.\n- The non-technical teacher is unlikely to edit the YAML.\n\nStill, a shipped config line that promises an effect and does nothing is a genuine defect, and the fix is one line: let the config value win when it is set and not 'auto', or delete line 169."

GPT weights are always named `<exp>-e<N>.ckpt` (see GPT_SoVITS/s1_train.py). SoVITS weights are `<exp>_e<N>_s<step>.pth`, so the step count, and therefore the name, is the same whenever clip count and batch size don't change, which is the case after proofreading. `_archive_old_run` moves only logs_s2/logs_s1.         "evidence": "I tried to refute this and could not. The defect is real, but it only shows up when the retrained model ends up with the same selected checkpoint and speed as before, which is likely but not guaranteed.\n\nWhat the code does:\n- Weight file names repeat between runs. The real GPT-SoVITS names SoVITS weights `name + \"_e%s_s%s\" % (epoch, global_step)` (ref/GPT-SoVITS/GPT_SoVITS/s2_train.py:569) and GPT weights `%s/%s-e%s.ckpt` (s1_train.py:75). GPT names never include the step count, so they collide on every retrain. SoVITS names collide whenever epochs and batches per epoch stay the same. Text-only proofreading changes neither. The bucket sampler pads each bucket to the batch size, so removing a few clips often leaves the step count unchanged too.\n- `_archive_old_run` (gptsovits.py:821-861) moves only `logs_s2_<v>`, `logs_s1_<v>` and `logs_s1`. It never touches `SoVITS_weights_<v>` or `GPT_weights_<v>`. The audit plan (ux_audit.json, item \"Retraining after adding material…\") explicitly said to move `SoVITS_weights_<v>/<exp>_e*.pth` and `GPT_weights_<v>/<exp>-e*.ckpt` into old_runs too, and that part was never implemented.\n- `model_id()` (gptsovits.py:1126-1128) hashes only the selected id and the two paths. `train()` sets selected to `s{last}-g{last}` with the same paths. The cache key at synth/engine.py:476 depends on `model_id()`, and a cache hit (engine.py:279) only checks that the wav and json files exist. The web UI has no way to clear the cache; `clear_cache` is reachable only from the CLI (cli.py:551).\n\nExperiment: I ran scratch test review/sk1_retrain/test_sk1_retrain.py with python3, using the repo's fake GPT-SoVITS. So the new run overwrote the old weights in place and the cache key did not change. The existing test `test_retrain_after_material_change_starts_fresh` only covers the case where clip count and step count change. It even asserts that old model files survive, which the GPT `.ckpt` files never do.\n\nLimits on the impact:\n1. In the real flow, `run_train` runs `run_select` afterwards (workflows.py:475-483). The cache only goes stale if that picks the same s/g combination out of the 12 candidates. With nearly identical data and GPT-SoVITS's fixed training seed, that is plausible but not certain.\n2. The speed calibration is part of the key (`round(speed, 3)`). select.py:224-225 sets it to 1.0 when the duration ratio is within 3%, so it often stays equal. When it falls outside 3%, the value will probably differ and the cache misses.\n3. The bug itself predates this release, but it only matters now. Before v0.1.6 a retrain resumed the old run and produced no new weights; v0.1.6 adds training from scratch.\n\nSecond claim (retrain stopped partway): also confirmed by reading the code. `train()` raises before `update_models`, so models.json keeps the old run's path lists and selected pair. `checkpoints()` prefers those lists, and `_locate_weight` only checks that a file exists. 重新挑选 can therefore mix new and old weights under the old names. If the stop happens during the GPT stage, the current selected pair is already new SoVITS plus old GPT, under an unchanged `model_id`.\n\nWhen it triggers, this is exactly what the teacher hits in step 4 of the scenario: after retraining, every sentence of a lesson she already generated comes back as 已有，直接用 with the old model's audio. Severity is major-to-moderate depending on how often selection repeats. Suggested fix, as the finding says: archive or rename this exp's weight files when a fresh run starts, and/or include st_size and st_mtime_ns (or a content hash) of the weight files in `model_id()`.\n---\nI could not refute it. I reproduced it end to end at HEAD 83bff05.\n\nCode:\n- `_archive_old_run` (gptsovits.py:821) moves only `logs_s2_<v>`, `logs_s1_<v>` and `logs_s1`. It never touches `SoVITS_weights_<v>/<exp>_e*_s*.pth` or `GPT_weights_<v>/<exp>-e*.ckpt`.\n- `model_id()` (line 1126-1128) hashes only the selected id and the two path strings.\n- The engine cache key (synth/engine.py:476) uses `model_id()` plus text, ref id and similar inputs. It includes nothing about file contents or mtime, and no code path clears `cache/segments` on retrain. `clear_cache` is called only from the CLI.\n\nReal GPT-SoVITS naming:\n- `s2_train.py:569` names files `name_e{epoch}_s{global_step}`. `s1_train.py:75` names them `<exp>-e<N>.ckpt`.\n- The s2 dataset (module/data_utils.py TextAudioSpeakerLoader) drops items only for a missing phoneme entry or for duration, and pads small sets to 100 items. Fixing a clip's text therefore leaves the item count, the bucket batches and global_step unchanged.\n- So the new run writes the exact same filenames and overwrites the old weights.\n\nExperiment (py3.11, fake GPT-SoVITS; the gsv39 venv fails on yaml in the fake subprocess, a known artifact). Script: <草稿目录>/review/sk2_retrain/test_sk2.py.\n- Steps: train with select=False, narrate one sentence, `set_clip_text` on one clip (the same path the new 采用建议 feature uses), retrain, narrate again.\n- Results:\n  - The log printed '检测到素材有变化：这次会从头训练新模型' and old_runs/ was created.\n  - The first and second SoVITS lists were identical (`vt_..._e1_s14` .. `e4_s56`), and all 8 weight mtimes changed, so the files were overwritten.\n  - `selected` was `s4-g4` both times with the same paths, and model_id was `gsv-v2ProPlus-s4-g4-d030de` both times.\n  - The second narration logged '[1/1] 已有，直接用' / '全部用的是之前生成好的结果', with cached=True.\n\nWhy this matters for this release:\n- The from-scratch retrain is new in v0.1.6 (`RUN_STAMP`, `_archive_old_run` and `_pick_run` were all added in the diff vs b518011). Typo fixing with an unchanged clip count (R5 proofcheck plus 采用建议) is exactly the workflow this release adds.\n- The audit plan (ux_audit.json, the \"archive the previous run\" proposal) explicitly said to move `SoVITS_weights_<v>/<exp>_e*.pth` and `GPT_weights_<v>/<exp>-e*.ckpt` into old_runs. The implementation skipped that.\n- The repo's own test, `test_retrain_after_material_change_starts_fresh`, asserts '旧素材的模型文件还在硬盘上（以前选中的模型不会突然消失）'. It only passes because it deletes 3 clips, which changes the step count. A text-only edit breaks this.\n\nLimits:\n- With select=True (the UI default), real auto-selection may pick a different combo, and then model_id changes. Even so, the fallback when selection fails picks the last epoch, which gives the same id. Re-picking the same id is also quite likely, since all 12 candidate paths are byte-identical across runs.\n- Whenever the id matches, narrations come back stale, or mix old-model and new-model sentences in one lesson. The old model is lost in every case.\n- The second problem (a stopped retrain) is also real. models.json keeps the old run's paths, some of which now hold new-run weights. For example, if SoVITS finishes and the run is stopped during GPT, the selected pair becomes new SoVITS plus old GPT under an unchanged model_id.\n\nA user-side workaround exists: the 只重新生成第几句 field forces regeneration. But the teacher has no way to know the cached audio is stale.\n---\nI tried to refute this and couldn't. The finding holds, with one caveat about how often the stale-cache case happens.\n\nWhat the code does:\n- `model_id()` (voicetwin/backends/gptsovits.py:1126-1128) hashes only the selected id and the two weight path strings.\n- `_archive_old_run` (lines 821-865) moves only logs_s2_<v>, logs_s1_<v> and logs_s1. SoVITS_weights_<v> and GPT_weights_<v> are never touched.\n- In the reference source, s2_train.py:569 names SoVITS weights `name_e{epoch}_s{global_step}`. The step count comes from len(train_loader), so it doesn't change when the clip count and batch size stay the same. s1_train.py:75 names GPT weights `-e{epoch}.ckpt`.\n- `plan_training` sets epochs from minutes and clip count only. `suspects` only switches DPO on or off. So after a text-only proofread, a fresh run produces exactly the same file names.\n\nHow the stale model reaches narration:\n- The engine cache key (voicetwin/synth/engine.py:476) is built from CACHE_VERSION, model_id, text, lang, ref id, aux ids, speed, tier settings and seed.\n- Nothing clears cache/segments after training. `project.update_models` just merges the JSON. `clear_cache` is called only from the CLI (cli.py:551), never from the web UI or from run_train.\n\nExperiment: scratchpad/review/sk3_retrain/test_sk3.py, run with py3.11 and the fake GPT-SoVITS.\n1. Retrain.\n\nResults:\n- old_runs/ was created and every weight file's mtime changed.\n- The SoVITS and GPT file name sets were identical to the first run.\n- `selected` stayed s4-g4 at the same paths.\n- model_id was `gsv-v2ProPlus-s4-g4-6fec01` both times.\n\nSo the old run's weight files are overwritten in place. That contradicts the existing test's own comment in tests/test_gptsovits_fake.py (\"旧素材的模型文件还在硬盘上（以前选中的模型不会突然消失）\"). That test only passes because it deletes 3 clips, which changes the step count.\n\nCaveat: the web UI always runs auto-select after training (run_train with select=True). The cache key only stays the same when the new selection lands on the same s?-g? combo and the speed calibration rounds to the same value (for example 1.0). The same happens when auto-select fails and both runs fall back to the last epoch. So the stale '已有，直接用' outcome is likely but not certain on every retrain.\n\nWhy it matters for this release: this release adds fresh retraining after material changes, and the UI tells the teacher to proofread (校对) to improve quality. In b518011, training resumed from the old logs instead of starting over. In the real flow (proofread, retrain, regenerate the same lesson or 鉴别 test), the teacher can silently get the old model's audio. The web UI offers no way to clear the cache.\n\nThe partial-retrain point also follows from the code. `checkpoints()` prefers the models.json lists, and those are rewritten only when training finishes, so after a stopped run they can mix files from two runs. That is a secondary edge case.\n\nThe fix is cheap: also hash each weight file's size and mtime in model_id, or put the run stamp into the archived or renamed weights."

The output path is fixed when 生成 is clicked, and a second 生成 can't start while the first is running (`_other_task` busy check). So a collision needs one whole run to start, finish, and be clicked again within the same clock minute.\n2. Every run that needs new audio starts the GPT-SoVITS api_v2 server and stops it afterwards. `run_narrate` creates its own backend and calls `backend.stop()`, `start()` launches a fresh `api_v2.py` and loads the models, and `stop()` waits up to 10 s. On a GPU the default tier is 完美 (≥8GB) or 极致 (`recommended_quality`), which takes minutes per lecture. A run under 60 s only happens on 快速 or 均衡 with a very short text.\n3. The finding's scenario, changing speed and generating again, is what the separate 「▶ 试听语速」 button is for. `do_speed_preview` writes to `cache_dir/speed_preview/语速_±N.wav`, not to outputs/.\n4. Fast reruns where every sentence is already cached skip the engine (`need_engine` in `synthesize_all`), so they don't need the server. In those cases the result is either identical (nothing changed) or differs only in extension (wav to mp3, so the first .wav survives). Changing the name gives a different stem, so no collision.\n5. Nothing is permanently lost. Each sentence is cached under a key built from text, ref, speed, tier and seed, so clicking again with the old settings rebuilds the overwritten version in seconds. When she fixes a typo and regenerates within the minute, replacing the old file is arguably what she wants.\n6. The claim that the first result's player and download list now point at the second audio doesn't hold. There is only one 结果 player, one file list and one `gen_state`, and the second run replaces all of them with its own result anyway.\n7. The 完美-variant leftover case needs a 完美 run to finish in under 60 s, which only happens when every sentence is already cached, followed immediately by a run on a different tier. That is very unlikely.\n\nIt is a cheap fix (add seconds or a `_2` suffix) and would be a fine follow-up. But it is a low-probability edge case, not a defect that matters for v0.1.6.\n---\nThe naming itself is as described. voicetwin/webui/app.py:1181-1186 builds `<stem>_%m月%d日%H点%M分.<fmt>` with no check for an existing file, and engine.narrate (voicetwin/synth/engine.py:795-841) writes the audio, .srt, .report.json and the 完美 variant files over whatever is there. So the code is what was specified, not a slip.\n\nIn practice the overwrite almost never loses anything:\n1) The name is fixed when a run starts, so a collision needs run 2 to start in the same clock minute that run 1 started.\n2) Any run whose audio would differ needs the engine. Redo, a speed change, a tier change and a text change all qualify, because speed, tier signature, text and seed are all in the cache key (engine.py:476-477). In that case synthesize_all calls _ensure_started(0.0) plus _warm_up for the scoring models (engine.py:732-736).\n3) Each run uses its own backend and stops it at the end (workflows.run_narrate `finally: backend.stop()`). GPTSoVITSBackend.start (gptsovits.py:1161-1210) therefore cold-starts api_v2.py every time and loads BERT, HuBERT, GPT and SoVITS. That takes tens of seconds; the log even prints \"启动中……已等待 N 秒\" every 15 s.\n4) So for run 2 to start in the same minute, run 1's cold start, synthesis, assembly, the teacher's listening and the re-click would all have to fit in under 60 s, inside one clock minute. On 完美 (many candidates, a denoise pass, two variants scored) that is even less realistic, so the \"orphaned variant files\" case is essentially theoretical.\n5) The only collision that happens easily is a re-run with nothing changed: every segment is cached, so no engine starts and it finishes in seconds. It's not worth fixing for this release."

        "evidence": "I tried to refute this and couldn't. The bug is real and reachable, but it's minor and rare.\n\nWhat the code does:\n- `synthesize_segment` with force=True (`voicetwin/synth/engine.py` around lines 561-570 and 672) only adds a random number to the seed. It keeps the same `plan.key` and overwrites `plan.wav_path` with `save_audio(plan.wav_path, best.wav, best.sr)`. The cache key in `_plan` (line 476) has no per-take seed.\n- No code in `voicetwin/` deletes `.dn*.wav` files. The only other `.dn` reference is the file counter at line 1105.\n- `_denoised` (lines 960-977) checks the cache only with `s == sr and len(w) == len(wav)`.\n\nHow a user hits it: the redo box in the web UI (`c[\"redo\"]` at app.py:2677) and the CLI `--redo` both go through `workflows.narrate` → `Narrator.narrate` → `synthesize_all(redo)` → `_make_variants` → `_denoised(r, ...)`. Here `r.path` is the same `plan.wav_path` as before the redo, so the old `.dnSR.wav` from the earlier run is found and reused if the length matches.\n\nExperiment (`scratchpad/review/dn_stale.py`, gsv39 venv): I saved take A, called `_denoised`, which wrote `abc.dn32000.wav`. I then overwrote `abc.wav` with a different take B of the same length and called `_denoised` again. The result correlated 0.998 with A and 0.000 with B, so the old take came back.\n\nHow likely it is: without clamping, the length after `trim_edges` is exactly (v1-v0)*hop + 2*half + 2*pad. That means two takes have equal length only when their voiced spans match to the 5 ms frame (160 samples at 32 kHz). GPT-SoVITS takes of one sentence usually vary by a few hundred ms in duration, so I estimate about 0.5-2% per redone sentence. It isn't hypothetical, though. The flow invites redos: the summary says \"可以只重做这几句\" and the CLI prints \"--redo ...\" for flagged sentences. So over many redos it will eventually happen.\n\nWhat happens when it does: version B silently keeps the rejected take, for example one with a misread word. B is also scored on that old audio, so the A/B recommendation is based on wrong data. If B is recommended or chosen, `<stem>.wav` undoes the redo for that sentence. Timing and subtitles still line up because the lengths match, so nothing looks wrong.\n\nThe fix is cheap: delete `<key>.dn*.wav` in `synthesize_segment` right after `save_audio`, or put the seed or a content hash in the denoise cache name.\n\nVerdict: a real correctness bug, minor severity, low probability.\n---\nI tried to refute this and couldn't. The mechanism is real and reachable, but it only fires on roughly 1 in 50 to 1 in 200 redone sentences.\n\nHow it happens (voicetwin/synth/engine.py at HEAD; the .dn cache is new in this release according to git diff b518011):\n- `_plan` builds the cache key at L475-477 from CACHE_VERSION, model, text, lang, ref, aux, speed, tier_sig and base_seed. The random offset that `force` adds to the seed (L568-569, `seed0 += random.randint(...)`) is not in the key, so a redo saves its new take over the same `plan.wav_path` (L692, `save_audio(plan.wav_path, best.wav, best.sr)`).\n- Nothing deletes or checks the `<key>.dn{sr}.wav` next to it. Searching for unlink/rmtree finds only `clear_cache` (L1103-1107), which removes the whole segments folder and is not part of the redo path.\n- `_denoised` (L960-976) reuses the cached file if it loads with `s == sr and len(w) == len(wav)`. It never compares the content.\n- `narrate` (L781 → L794) calls `synthesize_all(segments, redo)` and then `_make_variants`, so in the same run the new take is denoised against the old cache file. It stays stale until a later redo happens to produce a different length.\n\nHow likely a length match is:\n- `trim_edges` cuts on a 5 ms grid. Its length is (voiced[-1]-voiced[0])*hop plus a constant, and hop is 160 samples at 32 kHz.\n- I checked this by running the real `trim_edges` in the py3.9 venv with PYTHONPATH set to the repo (scratchpad/review/dn_stale.py). On simulated takes of one 3 s sentence (duration σ = 0.25 s), the minimum step between lengths was exactly 160 samples.\n- In that simulation two independent takes had the same length about 0.6% of the time. Short sentences, or takes that vary less, push this toward 1-3%.\n\nWho it affects:\n- Only the 完美 tier, since `\"variants\": True` is set only in `QUALITY_PRESETS[\"perfect\"]`.\n- It changes the final file only when version B is recommended or chosen.\n\nWhat goes wrong when it fires:\n- Version B silently contains the rejected old take for that sentence. Requirement R-variants says B must be \"the same selected candidates, each lightly denoised\", so this breaks it.\n- The per-sentence table shows the new take's scores, so the report doesn't match the audio.\n- If B is the final version, the teacher's redo is undone without any sign.\n\nSo it is not theoretical, just infrequent. Severity minor is right. The fix is one line: delete `<key>.dn*.wav` when the new wav is saved at L692, or put `best.seed` or a content hash in the cache file name.\n---\nI could not refute it. The code is as described, and an experiment reproduces the bug.\n\nCode (HEAD 83bff05, <仓库>/voicetwin/synth/engine.py):\n- `_plan` (around line 476): the cache key is short_hash(CACHE_VERSION, model, text, lang, ref, aux, speed, tier_sig, base_seed). The redo seed is not in it.\n- `synthesize_segment(force=True)` (561-570) only adds a random offset to seed0. It then writes `save_audio(plan.wav_path, ...)` at line 687, to the same path as before.\n- Nothing anywhere in the repo deletes `<key>.dn*.wav`. The only other `.dn` reference is the file count at line 1105.\n- `_denoised` (961-976) reuses the cache whenever `s == sr and len(w) == len(wav)`.\n- Redo is a promoted flow in this release. The UI has the textbox 只重新生成第几句 (app.py:2677), the CLI prints the `--redo` hint (cli.py:619), and the perfect tier flags sentences with 建议重新生成. Speech lengths of 2.000-2.039 s give 66880, 67040, ... 68160.\n- I built two different takes, A (220 Hz) and B (440 Hz, different lead and tail), whose trimmed length was the same 66880 samples. I saved A at the key path and ran `_denoised`, then overwrote the key wav with B as a redo would and ran `_denoised` again.\n- It returned the cached denoised A. That output correlates 1.0 with dn(A) and about 4e-7 with a fresh dn(B).\n\nSo whenever the redone take has the same trimmed length, version B silently contains the rejected old take. B is then scored on that old audio, and the final file reverts the redo if B is recommended or chosen. Meanwhile the results table shows the new take's percentage, so the user sees no sign of it.\n\nHow often: two takes of the same sentence from GPT-SoVITS typically differ in length by roughly 50-250 ms, which is 10-50 steps of 5 ms. That puts the chance of an exact collision at roughly 0.5-3% per redone sentence, higher for short sentences. It is rare, but it does happen in normal use, it is silent, and it undoes an action the user asked for.\n\nSeverity is minor and the fix is trivial: delete `plan.wav_path.stem + '.dn*.wav'` when `synthesize_segment` saves a new wav, or put the chosen seed or a hash of the source wav into the denoise cache name."

        "lens": "windows",
        "votes": "0/3",
        "confirmed": false,
        "evidence": "The finding rests on one premise: with bInheritHandles=False and no STARTF_USESTDHANDLES, the spawned child does not get the parent's stdout pipe. That premise is wrong for this case.\n\n1. How the child is started. Python 3.9 multiprocessing/popen_spawn_win32.py calls `_winapi.CreateProcess(python_exe, cmd, None, None, False, 0, env, None, None)`. Creation flags are 0, so the mp child attaches to s2_train's console (the windowless console that CREATE_NO_WINDOW gave s2_train in base.py run_logged). That is console mode \"Inherit\", not new-console and not detached. python.exe is a console-subsystem, 64-bit image.\n\n2. What Windows does in that case. Ryan Prichard's win32-console-docs is backed by a test suite (copy saved at scratchpad/review/win32-console-docs.md). Its \"CreateProcess (modern)\" section, Windows 8+, rule 6 says: when bInheritHandles is false, STARTF_USESTDHANDLES is not set, and the console mode is Inherit, \"The parent's standard handle is duplicated.\" The traditional XP–7 section, rule 5, says the same for non-console handles such as pipes and files: \"Windows duplicates each of the parent's non-console standard handles into the child.\" eryksun says the same on the Python tracker (bugs.python.org msg302493): console apps such as python.exe \"duplicate their standard handles into a child console process when the bInheritHandles argument of CreateProcess is false\". So the mp child's stdout and stderr are duplicates of the pipe write end that run_logged created, not \"the hidden console\".\n\n3. A sanity check. CPython 3.7+ subprocess.Popen on Windows with no redirection calls CreateProcess with bInheritHandles = int(not close_fds) = 0 and no STARTF_USESTDHANDLES (subprocess.py:1375-1439). These are exactly the parameters multiprocessing uses. If the finding were right, every `subprocess.run([...])` made from a Python process whose stdout is a pipe (CI runners, pip-captured setup.py builds) would lose the child's output on Windows. That does not happen.\n\n4. Flushing. The epoch lines ('Train Epoch: N [p%]' at s2_train.py:455 and '====> Epoch: N' at :579) go through logger.info. GPT_SoVITS/utils.py:19 sets up `logging.basicConfig(stream=sys.stdout)`, and StreamHandler flushes after every record. So even with a pipe as stdout, block buffering does not hold the lines back. The child's environment is inherited too (env=None, or a copy of os.environ).\n\nResult: on Windows the lines do reach run_logged's PIPE, `_sovits_parser` (gptsovits.py:395) sees them, and the SoVITS progress bar works. The only exceptions the docs list are XP's read-end-of-pipe bug and WOW64 32-to-32-bit, and neither applies to 64-bit runtime\\python.exe on Win10/11. The defect as described does not occur.\n---\nThe finding rests on a wrong model of how Windows CreateProcess handles standard handles. I could not run this on Windows: there is no Windows machine or wine here. The code paths and the documented platform behaviour still refute it.\n\n1. The mp child is created like this in Lib/multiprocessing/popen_spawn_win32.py (py3.9): `_winapi.CreateProcess(python_exe, cmd, None, None, False, 0, env, None, None)`. That means bInheritHandles=FALSE, creation flags 0 (no DETACHED_PROCESS, CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE or CREATE_NO_WINDOW), and no STARTUPINFO, so STARTF_USESTDHANDLES is not set. In that situation CreateProcess has a special case for standard handles. When the child is a console program (runtime\\python.exe) that attaches to the parent's console, Windows copies the parent's standard handles into the child, and when bInheritHandles is FALSE it duplicates them (BasepDuplicateAndWriteHandle). bInheritHandles does not block this.\n\n2. The condition holds for this chain. VoiceTwin starts s2_train with STARTF_USESTDHANDLES (subprocess sets it because stdout=PIPE and stderr=STDOUT, base.py:277) and CREATE_NO_WINDOW. That gives s2_train a hidden console, and its standard output and error are the VoiceTwin pipe. The mp child uses flags 0, so it attaches to that same hidden console and gets s2_train's standard handles duplicated, i.e. the pipe. CREATE_NO_WINDOW appears only one level up, where STARTF_USESTDHANDLES is in effect, so it does not matter here.\n\n3. Ordinary Python on Windows already depends on this exact pattern. `subprocess.Popen(cmd)` with no redirection gives use_std_handles=False and close_fds=True, which means bInheritHandles=int(not close_fds)=0 (subprocess.py:1373-1439). On 3.6 and earlier close_fds was also True by default when nothing was redirected. That is the same call as multiprocessing. Even so, the grandchild's output reaches a parent whose stdout is a pipe. For example, cl.exe compile errors started by distutils/setuptools spawn (subprocess.Popen(cmd)) appear in pip's captured \"N lines of output\". Output from `subprocess.check_call` tools also shows up in Jenkins and GitHub Actions logs on Windows. And prints from multiprocessing children show up in PyCharm's piped Run window on Windows. If the finding were right, all of these would be lost.\n\n4. The rest of the path is fine. s2_train's run() logs through utils.py:19 `logging.basicConfig(stream=sys.stdout, level=INFO)`, which is re-imported in the spawned child. The child inherits the environment (env=None), and that environment includes PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 from subprocess_env() (worker.py:33). So the 'Train Epoch: N [p%]' and '====> Epoch: N' lines are written to the inherited pipe without block buffering, and VoiceTwin's reader feeds them to _sovits_parser.\n\nThe claimed stall for the whole SoVITS stage (no progress, then the amber bar) therefore does not happen for this reason. Tailing train.log would be optional extra safety, not a fix for a real Windows defect.\n---\nRefuted. The finding relies on the STARTUPINFO docs (\"otherwise the default is the console window's buffer\"), but Windows does not behave that way. If a console-subsystem child is created without STARTF_USESTDHANDLES, without DETACHED_PROCESS or CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE, and with bInheritHandles=FALSE, Windows copies the parent's standard handles into the child's process parameters and duplicates them into the child. Pipes and files are included, not only console handles. Windows 8+ does this with the PS_STD_HANDLE_INFO / PsRequestDuplicate mechanism.\n\nWhat I checked:\n\n1. Python 3.9 stdlib, subprocess.py lines 1373-1439. On Windows, a plain `subprocess.run(cmd)` with no redirection skips STARTF_USESTDHANDLES. Because close_fds defaults to True, it passes `int(not close_fds)` = 0 as bInheritHandles. Creation flags are 0. These are exactly the arguments popen_spawn_win32.py passes: `CreateProcess(python_exe, cmd, None, None, False, 0, env, None, None)`. If the finding were right, the output of every un-redirected subprocess launched by a Python process whose stdout is a pipe or file would disappear on Windows. That would break build tools and CI logs everywhere. In practice that output reaches the pipe.\n\n2. Wine models this behavior because Windows applications depend on it. I fetched the current Wine source into scratchpad/review/.\n   - dlls/kernelbase/process.c:206-217. Without STARTF_USESTDHANDLES, and without DETACHED_PROCESS or CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE, it sets `params->hStdOutput = NtCurrentTeb()->Peb->ProcessParameters->hStdOutput` (the parent's).\n   - server/process.c:1309-1318. When the INHERIT_HANDLES flag is not set and the child is not allocating a new console, it runs `info->data->hstdout = duplicate_handle(parent, info->data->hstdout, process, ...)`.\n   - kernelbase lines 195-202. CREATE_NO_WINDOW gives s2_train a windowless console. The grandchild, created with flags 0, inherits that console, so the duplication path applies.\n\n3. Applied to VoiceTwin: run_logged (base.py:277) starts s2_train with stdout=PIPE, so s2_train's own standard handle is VoiceTwin's pipe. The mp.spawn child of s2_train gets a duplicate of that pipe. The child's env is inherited (popen_spawn_win32 passes env=None for the embedded runtime python). That env includes PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1, PYTHONIOENCODING and PYTHONUTF8 from worker.py:33. utils.py:19 calls `logging.basicConfig(stream=sys.stdout)`, and the StreamHandler flushes after every record. So the 'Train Epoch: N [p%]' and '====> Epoch: N' logger lines reach VoiceTwin's pipe, and the `print('start training from epoch')` line does too. _sovits_parser (gptsovits.py:395) therefore sees them on Windows as well.\n\nThe claim that the child's output goes to the hidden console, leaving the bar stuck for the whole SoVITS stage, comes from misreading the documentation. It is not real behavior. No fix is needed for this release."

        "evidence": "The underlying mechanism is real. Children are started with CREATE_NO_WINDOW, there is no Job Object, the task threads are daemon threads, and there is no atexit or console-ctrl handler. So closing the console mid-task kills VoiceTwin without running the `finally: backend.stop()`. But the finding's scenario and severity do not hold up, and the behaviour is not new in this release.\n\n1. **The premise is wrong.** api_v2 is not left running after \"the teacher generates audio\". The web UI keeps no long-lived backend; every `get_backend` call is in workflows.py. Each of run_narrate (workflows.py:557-565), run_select (500-505) and run_blind (718-736) creates its own backend and calls `backend.stop()` in `finally`. That sends /control exit and falls back to taskkill /T. So api_v2 exists only while a generate, select, speed-preview or blind-test task is running. Closing the window between tasks, which is the normal way to quit, leaves nothing behind. An orphan needs her to close the window mid-task. The UI tells her not to do that in several places, including the R1 amber bar text \"请先等一等，不要关闭黑色窗口\", and this release adds a real Stop button, which was the audit plan's chosen remedy for exactly this problem.\n\n2. **The orphan-training claim is very likely wrong.** The finding says s2_train keeps writing into logs/vt_xxx. But s2_train and s1_train write progress through tqdm to stdout/stderr, which is a PIPE read by VoiceTwin (base.py:277-278). VoiceTwin parses the \"Train Epoch\" lines, so rank 0's output reaches that pipe. When the parent dies, the next tqdm write or flush fails with EPIPE, or EINVAL on Windows. tqdm 4.70.1's DisableOnWriteError only swallows errno 5 (tqdm/utils.py:180-182), so the error propagates and the trainer exits. I reproduced this with the gsv39 venv (scratchpad/review/orphan_parent.py and orphan_child.py). The child piped through tqdm and logging died within a second of the parent being SIGKILLed, with \"DIED BrokenPipeError [Errno 32]\". So the \"two trainers in one directory\" scenario does not happen. I ran this on Linux; on Windows the errno differs but is still not 5.\n\n3. **Training is not wrecked by the remaining case.** That case is an idle api_v2 left over from a window closed mid-generation. It writes to a log file, so it does survive, and it is later adopted and never stopped (self.proc stays None). But `train()` calls `_gpu_memory()`, which calls `gpu_status(refresh=True)`, and `plan_training` deliberately sizes the batch to the free VRAM when the card is occupied (gptsovits.py:267-287). Its comment cites exactly the Windows sysmem-fallback risk, and it logs a note. `_with_oom_retry` halves the batch on OOM. After training, selection and generation work normally through the adopted server. The cost is roughly 1.5–3 GB of VRAM and a smaller batch, so somewhat slower training, until she logs off or shuts down. That kills the user-session process, including with Fast Startup.\n\n4. **It is not a regression.** b518011 (v0.1.3) has the same CREATE_NO_WINDOW Popen and the same adopt-alive `start()` (`if self._alive(): ... return` at old line 449). None of R1–R9 asks for process-tree lifetime management.\n\nVerdict: this is an existing hardening gap with a narrow trigger (closing the window mid-task against on-screen instructions). The worst sub-scenario is refuted, and the leftover impact is limited and already partly handled. Assigning api_v2 to a KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE Job Object, and stopping an adopted server before training, would be nice follow-ups, but this is not a major defect to fix for this release.\n---\nThe training half of the claim is most likely wrong. The api_v2 half is real but narrow, it is softened by existing code, it predates this release, and the plan deferred it on purpose.\n\n1) Orphaned training processes (s2_train, its mp.spawn ranks, s1_train). run_logged (voicetwin/backends/base.py:270-277) starts training with stdout=PIPE and stderr=STDOUT, and the parent reads that pipe. The rank processes do write into it: the s2 progress parser _sovits_parser (gptsovits.py:985-986) depends on their output arriving there. GPT-SoVITS rank 0 writes tqdm(train_loader) on every batch (ref s2_train.py:331), and Lightning's tqdm bar does the same in s1_train. When VoiceTwin dies, the read end of the pipe closes. The next tqdm flush then raises an OSError. tqdm's DisableOnWriteError (tqdm/utils.py:180-182) only swallows errno 5, so the error escapes the training loop and the rank crashes, which brings mp.spawn down too. I tested this in the gsv39 venv with scratchpad/review/orphan_parent.py and orphan_child.py: I SIGKILLed the piped parent, and the tqdm child died within seconds with BrokenPipeError(32). On Windows the error is EINVAL or EPIPE, and tqdm re-raises that the same way. So the scenario where \"the orphan s2_train keeps writing into logs/vt_xxx while a second training starts\" is not realistic. Training dies within about one batch and resumes from its checkpoint next time.\n\n2) Orphaned api_v2. This part is true: its stdout goes to a file (gptsovits.py:1185-1189), so nothing kills it. But:\n- (a) api_v2 only runs during a task. run_select, run_narrate and the blind test all call backend.stop() in a finally block (workflows.py:505, 564, 736), and the web UI never keeps a backend alive between clicks (no backend= in webui/*.py). The plan's deferred list explicitly rejected keeping the server alive for exactly this reason. Closing the window after work is finished, which is the normal way to exit, leaves no orphan. An orphan needs the window closed in the middle of a generation. The UI says not to do that in several places: app.py:63 and 2627, the amber bar at progress.py:573/576, and 快速上手.md:42 (\"黑色窗口不要关\"). There is also a Stop button that runs backend.stop().\n- (b) The effect on the next training is already handled. plan_training (gptsovits.py:266-286) computes the batch size from free VRAM when another program is holding memory. Its comment is written for exactly this Windows case: \"Windows 显存满了不报错，而是慢好几倍\". It also tells her that some GB are in use, and _with_oom_retry halves the batch if memory still runs out. The realistic worst case is a somewhat slower training, not OOM or a failure.\n- (c) The behaviour is not new. Adopting a server that is already running at start() is the same in the older build/lib/voicetwin/backends/gptsovits.py. ux_audit.json plan.deferred explicitly postpones \"cleanup of api_v2.py servers left over from earlier sessions (pid file plus /control?command=exit): both need real Windows testing\".\n\nWhat remains is a known, deliberately deferred limitation of moderate impact, not a genuine defect for this release.\n---\nThe finding is mostly overstated or wrong. Only the api_v2 part holds up, and that part is old behaviour whose effect the code already softens.\n\n1) Training children do not survive. This is the \"orphan s2_train keeps writing into logs/vt_xxx\" scenario. run_logged (base.py:277) starts s1_train and s2_train with stdout=PIPE and stderr=STDOUT, so VoiceTwin owns the only read end. Once VoiceTwin dies, the next write fails: EPIPE on Linux, ERROR_NO_DATA→OSError errno 22 on Windows.\n- s2_train's ranks write a tqdm bar every batch (ref s2_train.py:331).\n- s1_train writes Lightning's bar, which _gpt_parser reads from that same pipe.\n- tqdm's DisableOnWriteError swallows only errno 5 (tqdm 4.70.1 utils.py:180-182). Any other errno is re-raised, so the rank crashes and mp.spawn's join takes down the main process.\n- I ran it in the py3.9 venv (scratchpad/review/orph). A parent Popen with stdout=PIPE ran trainer.py, which uses an mp spawn rank, a tqdm loop and logging to stdout. After kill -9 of the parent, the progress file stopped growing. Within 5 s both the main and rank processes were dead.\n- The ranks have to hold the inherited pipe. If their stderr were None, tqdm would raise AttributeError right away (I checked this), and training under VoiceTwin would never work at all.\n- So \"two trainings in the same exp dir\" is refuted.\n\n2) api_v2 does outlive the console. Its stdout goes to a file, it is started with CREATE_NO_WINDOW, and there is no job object. start() also adopts an alive server without recording it, so stop() never ends it.\n- This is not new in this release. The b518011 version has the same start()/stop()/creationflags code (lines 448-451, 468-472).\n- It needs a narrow trigger. api_v2 runs only during an active generate, select or speed task. The web UI passes no backend, so run_narrate and run_select call backend.stop() in finally (workflows.py:505, 564). Quitting normally after a task leaves nothing behind. Only closing the window mid-generation or mid-selection does, and both the window title and the amber stall text tell her not to.\n- The impact is already handled. Before training, plan_training sizes the batch from measured free VRAM: gpu_status free_gb, device-wide, plus FREE_SLACK_GB (gptsovits.py:267-287, 736-746). It logs that other programs hold VRAM, which avoids the WDDM sysmem-fallback slowdown. _with_oom_retry (938-957) halves the batch on OOM. So training still finishes, maybe somewhat slower. Generation just reuses the adopted server, and a reboot clears it.\n\nThe audit plan deferred keeping the server alive for exactly this reason. It chose stop-after-every-task as the mitigation, and orphan cleanup was not a planned unit item.\n\nNet: a minor robustness gap, not a major defect for this release."

It matches the claimed layer1 activation of 256 ch x 80 x T x 4 bytes, with several such tensors live inside each bottleneck block.\n\n4) Process context. `similarity.device: auto` resolves to cuda in the web UI process. GPT-SoVITS inference runs in a separate api_v2.py subprocess (backends/gptsovits.py), which already holds VRAM. On a typical 8-12 GB card, any narration longer than about 2-5 minutes will either:\n- throw CUDA OOM, which is swallowed, leaving an empty ERes2NetV2 column and a 综合 % from resemblyzer alone while the sentence clips get the two-model average; or\n- on recent Windows NVIDIA drivers with the default sysmem fallback, spill into shared memory and run extremely slowly, failing anyway at 10+ minutes.\n\nAfter the OOM, the caching allocator in the web UI process keeps the blocks it reserved and never calls `empty_cache`. That VRAM is unavailable to the separate api_v2 process for the next generation. With no GPU, the CPU path tries to allocate about 19-29 GB of RAM for a 10-15 minute lecture.\n\n5) Nothing handles this elsewhere. The suggested fix is sound: embed about 10 s windows and use the L2-normalised mean embedding, and optionally call `torch.cuda.empty_cache()`.\n---\nI could not refute this. The code path is real, it runs by default, and nothing else in the code handles it.\n\n1. In the first block, these are alive at the same time:\n   - x (64 channels)\n   - spx/bn1 output (96)\n   - conv3 output and bn3 output (256 + 256)\n   - shortcut conv and bn outputs (256 + 256)\n   \n   That is roughly 600 to 950 channels × 80 × T × 4 bytes. It works out to about 1.2-1.8 GB per minute of audio:\n   - 10 minutes: about 12-18 GB\n   - 5 minutes: about 6-9 GB\n   \n   That is beyond a 12 GB card (the RTX 5070 used as the example in R2). The GPT-SoVITS worker is a separate process (backends/worker_backend.py) and may already be holding VRAM.\n\n3. The full-length files are in the default list. The failure is hidden. `SimilarityJudge.embed` (speaker.py:605-611) catches every Exception with `log.debug`. The sentence clips in the same ranked table use the two-model average.\n\n5. Nothing mitigates it. The only `torch.cuda.empty_cache` in the codebase is in data/proofcheck.py. `similarity.device` defaults to `auto`, which means cuda in the web UI process. Two consequences:\n   - When a long forward pass succeeds, PyTorch's caching allocator keeps many GB reserved inside the web UI process. The separate GPT-SoVITS worker can't use that VRAM for the next generation.\n   - On Windows, the sysmem-fallback / WDDM paging may make the pass extremely slow instead of failing.\n\n6. The only other callers of the judge (metrics.py scorer, synth/select.py) score one sentence at a time. It matters for this release: R5f/R8 ask for accurate per-model columns and a fair ranking. The fix the finding proposes (embed about 10 s windows and use the L2-normalised mean, then empty the CUDA cache) is the right one."

        "claim": "_output_path builds every web output filename with `time.strftime('%m月%d日%H点%M分')`. On Windows, CPython 3.9 does not use wcsftime (timemodule.c: `#ifdef MS_WINDOWS #undef HAVE_WCSFTIME`). It encodes the format with PyUnicode_EncodeLocale using the C-runtime LC_CTYPE codepage, which Python 3.9 sets from the user locale at startup. When the system's 'language for non-Unicode programs' is not Chinese (for example cp1252), '月' cannot be encoded and strftime raises UnicodeEncodeError.         "scenario": "A teacher uses an English-locale Windows laptop with Chinese display language, which leaves the system ANSI codepage at 1252. Every click on 生成 fails before synthesis with \"'locale' codec can't encode character '月'\".",
        "fix": "Build the suffix without CJK characters in the strftime format, for example `t = time.localtime(); f\"{t.tm_mon:02d}月{t.tm_mday:02d}日{t.tm_hour:02d}点{t.tm_min:02d}分\"`.",

        "confirmed": true,
        "evidence": "I could not refute this. The mechanism is real and checked against CPython 3.9 source (saved in scratchpad/review/). The scenario needs one correction, which narrows who is affected without making the bug go away.\n\n1. **The CJK format is new in this release.** `voicetwin/webui/app.py:1186` calls `time.strftime('%m月%d日%H点%M分')` inside `_output_path`. The diff against b518011 shows this line was added in this release; the old output naming in workflows.py:512 used ASCII `'%Y%m%d_%H%M%S'`. The only caller is `do_generate` at app.py:2165. It is not inside any try block and runs before the first progress yield, so an exception aborts every click on 生成 and Gradio shows a generic error.\n\n2. **CPython 3.9 really takes this path on Windows.** In Modules/timemodule.c (3.9), lines 684-686 read `#ifdef MS_WINDOWS /* wcsftime() doesn't format correctly time zones, see issue #10653 */ #undef HAVE_WCSFTIME`. So `time_strftime` runs `format = PyUnicode_EncodeLocale(format_arg, \"surrogateescape\")` (line 780). In fileutils.c, `encode_locale_ex` with current_locale=1 calls `encode_current_locale`, which uses `wcstombs` under the current C-runtime LC_CTYPE. UTF-8 mode is ignored on that path, so the `PYTHONUTF8` set in worker.py would not help even if the web UI used it. A character that cannot be encoded raises `UnicodeEncodeError: 'locale' codec can't encode character`.\n\n3. **LC_CTYPE comes from the user's Windows locale.** preconfig.c (3.9) calls `_Py_SetLocaleFromEnv(LC_CTYPE)`, i.e. `setlocale(LC_CTYPE, \"\")`, at startup. Nothing in the repo, GPT-SoVITS or gradio changes it later. matplotlib only calls setlocale when `axes.formatter.use_locale` is on, which is off by default. The launcher's `chcp 65001` only changes the console code page, not the C-runtime locale.\n\n4. **Correction to the scenario:** `setlocale(LC_CTYPE, \"\")` uses the user's Regional format, not the system ANSI code page (\"language for non-Unicode programs\") as the finding says. I checked which code pages can encode 月, 日, 点 and 分:\n   - **Works:** cp936 (Simplified Chinese, China) and cp932 (Japanese) encode all four.\n   - **Fails:** cp1252 (English or Western European) fails on all four. cp950 (Traditional Chinese, Taiwan or Hong Kong) fails on 点.\n\n**Who is affected:** a default mainland-China Windows install works. A teacher whose Regional format is English, European, or Taiwan/Hong Kong Chinese cannot generate any audio. Overseas or Hong Kong/Taiwan Chinese teachers are a realistic group. Linux tests cannot catch this because Linux uses wcsftime.\n\nI could not reproduce it on Linux for that same reason, so this conclusion rests on reading the source. It is a new regression that breaks the core feature for that group, and the fix is a one-line change that builds the CJK text outside strftime (from `time.localtime()` fields). Minor severity is fair, but it is worth fixing in v0.1.6.\n---\nI could not refute how the failure happens. The only weak point is how many users actually reach it.\n\n1) Read the CPython 3.9 source (fetched to scratchpad/review/timemodule39.c and fileutils39.c):\n- timemodule.c lines 684-687 contain `#ifdef MS_WINDOWS / #undef HAVE_WCSFTIME` (\"wcsftime() doesn't format correctly time zones, see issue #10653\").\n- So on Windows, time_strftime runs `format = PyUnicode_EncodeLocale(format_arg, \"surrogateescape\")` (line 780).\n- PyUnicode_EncodeLocale passes current_locale=1. In fileutils.c encode_locale_ex (lines 777-789), that goes straight to encode_current_locale (wcstombs under the CRT LC_CTYPE). UTF-8 mode and PYTHONUTF8 are ignored on this path, so `PYTHONUTF8=1` in worker.py would not help. It is only set for the worker subprocess anyway.\n- preconfig.c calls `_Py_SetLocaleFromEnv(LC_CTYPE)` at startup, which is setlocale(LC_CTYPE, \"\") and uses the system ANSI code page.\n- surrogateescape does not cover a real CJK character, so the call raises UnicodeEncodeError.\n\n2) Which code pages fail (tested with Python's codecs):\n- '%m月%d日%H点%M分' encodes fine in cp936, cp932, cp949 and utf-8.\n- It fails in cp1252 (on '月').\n- It also fails in cp950, the Traditional Chinese code page for Taiwan and Hong Kong, on the simplified '点' (U+70B9).\n\n3) Reachability: `_output_path` (app.py:1181-1186) is called in do_generate (app.py:2165) before any yield and with no try/except. On an affected machine, every web 生成 click raises before synthesis starts.\n\n4) It is a regression in this release. b518011 has no CJK strftime in webui/app.py. The pattern arrived in d22b52d. workflows.py:512 still uses the ASCII-safe '%Y%m%d_%H%M%S'.\n\n5) Why the impact is limited (the reason this is minor):\n- The target user is a mainland teacher: the app points to nvidia.cn, and config.py/project.py treat Notepad \"ANSI\" as GBK.\n- Mainland Windows has ACP 936, where the format encodes fine. So that teacher's own machine almost certainly works.\n- It does break for Traditional-Chinese Windows (cp950) users of the public release, and for any machine whose 'language for non-Unicode programs' is non-CJK.\n- No library loaded in the web UI process resets LC_CTYPE. matplotlib's setlocale is gated off by default.\n\nVerdict: a real latent crash, introduced in this release, with a trivial zero-risk fix (format the month, day, hour and minute numbers in Python instead of putting CJK in the strftime format). Severity minor, because the primary user's zh-CN system will not trigger it.\n---\nI tried to refute this and couldn't. The CPython 3.9 source backs up every step of the claim.\n\n1. In Modules/timemodule.c (3.9 branch, around line 685) there is `#ifdef MS_WINDOWS /* wcsftime() doesn't format correctly time zones, see issue #10653 */ # undef HAVE_WCSFTIME`. On Windows, time_strftime therefore runs `format = PyUnicode_EncodeLocale(format_arg, \"surrogateescape\")` (line 780).\n2. In Objects/unicodeobject.c:3692, PyUnicode_EncodeLocale calls unicode_encode_locale(..., current_locale=1). In Python/fileutils.c, encode_locale_ex with current_locale=1 goes straight to encode_current_locale, which calls wcstombs on each character using the C runtime's LC_CTYPE. UTF-8 mode and PYTHONUTF8 are never checked on this path. When wcstombs fails, the result is -2, and unicode_encode_locale raises UnicodeEncodeError('locale', ..., 'encoding error').\n3. Python/preconfig.c:812-813 calls _Py_SetLocaleFromEnv(LC_CTYPE) at startup (configure_locale=1 by default). On Windows that is setlocale(LC_CTYPE, \"\"), and the C runtime takes the code page from GetACP(), which is the system's \"language for non-Unicode programs\" setting.\n\nNothing in the repo or the runtime changes the locale. I found no setlocale call in voicetwin, the GPT-SoVITS reference, or gradio 4.24. `chcp 65001` in the launchers only changes the console code page, not the ANSI code page. The PYTHONUTF8 that worker.py sets applies only to the child processes it starts, and step 2 shows it would not help anyway.\n\nWhich code pages can encode the format string '%m月%d日%H点%M分' (checked in Python): cp936 OK, cp932 OK, cp949 OK, utf-8 OK. cp1252 and cp1250 FAIL on '月'. cp950 (Traditional Chinese Windows) FAILS on '点'.\n\nHow it reaches the user: _output_path (voicetwin/webui/app.py:1186) is called unconditionally in do_generate (app.py:2165), before any try or stream_task. On an affected machine, every click on 生成 raises before synthesis starts, and gradio shows a generic error. The Linux tests use glibc wcsftime, so they cannot catch this.\n\nIt is a regression in v0.1.6. The previous release (b518011) named web outputs through workflows.py with the ASCII format time.strftime('%Y%m%d_%H%M%S'). This CJK format string is new in this diff, and it is the only non-ASCII strftime format in the codebase.\n\nCaveat on likelihood: a typical mainland Chinese Windows install (ANSI code page 936) works fine. The failure needs English or Western-locale Windows (for example an English install with a Chinese display language pack) or Traditional Chinese Windows. That is uncommon for the target user, but when it happens the core feature fails every time, and the fix is a one-line f-string built from time.localtime() fields. Minor severity is right, and it is a genuine regression worth fixing before release."

        "severity": "major",
        "file": "voicetwin/utils/audio.py",

        "claim": "`auto_silence_threshold` drops every frame whose level is exactly -100 dB (`db = db[db > -100]`). `frame_rms_db` returns exactly -100.0 for all-zero frames (10*log10(0+1e-10)). GPT-SoVITS api_v2 adds `fragment_interval` 0.3 s of exact zeros after every sentence (TTS.audio_postprocess, and VoiceTwin sends fragment_interval=0.3), so that padding never counts toward the noise floor. When a generated sentence has little non-zero quiet audio of its own, the 3rd-percentile 'noise' comes from the speech itself, and the threshold rises to speech95-15 dB instead of speech95-30 dB. `trim_edges` (engine.py:154) then cuts real speech at the sentence edges. In `attempt()` the scoring and ASR/CER check run on the UNTRIMMED wav (engine.py:607) and only then is `trim_edges` applied, so the cut word is never detected. The same estimator feeds `speech_activity` (rate in Scorer, `in_normal_range`, and `select.py:186` `gen_voiced`), so the measured voiced time of clean generated audio comes out too short compared with the user's noisy real clips.",
        "scenario": "Measured: a 1.8 s run of loud syllables plus a final syllable 18 dB quieter, with no leading silence, followed by 0.3 s of exact zeros. thr=-26.3 dB; trim_edges output is 1.800 s out of 2.010 s of speech, so the final syllable (e.g. 了/吗/呢) is removed entirely. With the same tail at +/-1 LSB instead of exact zero: thr=-40 dB and nothing is lost (scratchpad/review/audio/trimtest3.py). With the dummy worker's speech at noise=0 plus the 0.3 s zero tail, measured voiced time drops 1.69 s -> 1.41 s (trimtest4.py). That makes the rate look ~20% too fast, so the 完美 tier's `in_normal_range` fails, it runs all 20 tries and flags the sentence 'may not sound like you'. It also pushes the select-time speed calibration (gen_voiced/real_voiced) below 1, which slows every narration.",
        "fix": "In `auto_silence_threshold`, count exact-zero frames as floor (-100) instead of discarding them, or for synthesized audio use a fixed relative threshold (e.g. speech95-40 dB).         "lens": "audio",

        "confirmed": false,
        "evidence": "I could not confirm this finding. The code path is real, but the failure only happens with audio that a neural vocoder does not actually produce.\n\nWhat checks out: `auto_silence_threshold` (voicetwin/utils/audio.py:112) drops frames at exactly -100 dB. GPT-SoVITS `TTS.audio_postprocess` does append `torch.zeros(sr*fragment_interval)`, and gptsovits.py:1289 sends fragment_interval=0.3. So the 0.3 s tail is digital zero and is left out of the noise estimate.\n\nWhy it is not a defect worth fixing for this release:\n\n1. The threshold only rises if almost none of the non-zero frames are quiet. Fewer than 3% of them may lie below speech95-36 dB. I added a short quiet segment before the zero tail of the reporter's own worst-case signal (8 loud vowel syllables, no consonants, a final syllable 18 dB quieter, no leading silence). Script: scratchpad/review/skeptic1/quiet_margin.py.\n   - The quiet was int16-quantized noise at -70, -80 or -90 dBFS, like a vocoder.\n   - 40 ms of it gives thr=-30.0 and a trimmed length of 2.000 s for 2.010 s of speech.\n   - 60 ms gives thr=-39.1 and keeps everything (2.040 s).\n   - 100 ms or more gives -40.0, the normal speech-30 value.\n   The level of the quiet audio makes no difference. Even at -90 dBFS (about 0.6 LSB RMS), `(audio*32768).astype(int16)` leaves non-zero samples in every 40 ms frame. So only output that is digitally silent apart from the speech triggers the problem.\n\n2. Real fine-tuned GPT-SoVITS output always has quiet frames well above that amount:\n   - VoiceTwin's slicer (voicetwin/data/slicer.py, max_sil_kept_ms=400) keeps half of each gap, capped at 0.4 s, on each side of every training clip. With min_interval 300 ms that is at least about 150 ms of room tone. Training clips are never trimmed afterwards; only references and the scoring copies are. So the fine-tuned model learns to produce leading and trailing near-silence, which comes out as low-level non-zero vocoder noise.\n   - Chinese stop and affricate closures and comma pauses inside the sentence add more quiet frames.\n   - 3% of a 2 s sentence is only about 6 frames (60 ms).\n\n3. The reporter's numbers come from audio that a vocoder does not produce:\n   - trimtest3 is a consonant-free train of vowel-only syllables with nothing quiet at either edge.\n   - trimtest4 runs the dummy worker with noise=0 (its default is 0.002), where comma pauses are exact zeros. A VITS or BigVGAN vocoder never outputs that.\n   With the dummy's default noise, thr is -40 to -42 dB, which is normal.\n\n4. The knock-on effects (rate and `in_normal_range` in the 完美 tier, and select.py `gen_voiced` calibration) all depend on the same estimator. They inherit the same precondition, so they do not appear on realistic output either.\n\nMaking exact-zero frames count as -100 dB would be a harmless one-line hardening. Without it, though, I see no realistic GPT-SoVITS output that loses a syllable or skews the rate, so this is theoretical, not a major release defect.\n---\nThe code reading is correct, but the failure only shows up with synthetic signals. I could not reproduce it with real speech.\n\n1. What the code does. `auto_silence_threshold` (voicetwin/utils/audio.py:106-119) does drop frames at exactly -100 dB, and api_v2 does append 0.3 s of exact zeros (TTS.audio_postprocess, TTS.py:1550-1560). Scoring also runs before `trim_edges` (engine.py:607-608).\n\n2. What it takes to trigger. The cap at speech95-15 dB only applies when fewer than about 3% of the non-zero frames sit more than about 21 dB below speech95. For a 2 s sentence that means under about 60 ms of quiet audio in total. The finder's own trimtest2.py shows that a 60 ms lead-in at -75 dB already brings thr back to -33/-39 dB and keeps the final syllable. The repro uses harmonic tones with a sin^0.5 envelope (inter-syllable dips are only about 8 dB) and the dummy worker with noise=0. Neither looks like real speech.\n\n3. Experiment with real speech (scratchpad/review/sk2/real1-3.py). I used CMU ARCTIC arctic_a0007.wav from the pysptk sdist, cut it tightly at speech95-25 dB so there is no leading or trailing silence at all, and appended 0.3 s of exact zeros. Results: p3=-42.2, thr=-36.2 (speech95-21). trim_edges kept 3.055 s of the 3.040 s core and voiced time was 3.03 s, the same as with a -70/-80 dB floor in place of the zeros. The speech's own stop closures and dips are deep enough to keep the threshold low.\n\n4. Harder version of the same test. I made the last 0.3 s 18 dB quieter, like a weak sentence-final particle. With the zero tail the trim kept 2.955 s; with a realistic -80 dB floor it kept 2.990 s. That is a 35 ms difference in a very weak tail, not a lost syllable. Voiced time was 2.92 s vs 2.90 s, so the claimed bias toward short voiced time (about 20%) does not appear. It only appeared when I zeroed every sample more than 25 dB below speech95, and no vocoder outputs that.\n\n5. Real GPT-SoVITS output is not digital silence. The neural vocoder leaves low-level output in silences and closures, and it learns the training clips' noise floor and the edge silence the slicer leaves on each clip. R5d itself says trim_edges exists because generated sentences leave \"residual hiss\" at their edges. So the 3rd percentile picks up that hiss, not the speech, and the threshold stays near speech95-30.\n\nChanging the -100 exclusion would be a harmless hardening, but this is not a major defect for this release.\n---\nI'm marking this not a real defect for this release. The mechanism exists in the code, but it only does harm in a signal that GPT-SoVITS doesn't produce, and the reproduction uses synthetic tones.\n\nWhat the report gets right:\n- `auto_silence_threshold` (voicetwin/utils/audio.py:112) does drop frames at exactly -100 dB.\n- api_v2 `audio_postprocess` (TTS.py:1550-1560) appends 0.3 s of exact zeros, and VoiceTwin sends fragment_interval=0.3.\n- `attempt()` scores the untrimmed wav and only then calls `trim_edges`.\n- I reran trimtest3.py and got the reported numbers (thr -26.3 dB, 1.800 s kept out of 2.010 s). That signal is 8 sine \"syllables\" with a sin^0.5 envelope, which gives almost no low-energy frames, plus a last syllable artificially 18 dB quieter.\n\nWhy it doesn't bite on real audio:\n1. **Only the zero padding is dropped.** A frame is excluded only when its whole 40 ms window is digital zero. Any vocoder noise counts, even 1 LSB (about -99.997 dB). HiFiGAN/VITS output is never exactly zero, and GPT-SoVITS copies the reference's background noise, which here comes from the teacher's real recordings.\n2. **The estimator gets its noise floor from the speech itself.** To raise the threshold, 97% of the non-zero frames would have to sit within 36 dB of the 95th-percentile level. Natural onsets, offsets, closures and fricatives break that.\n\nExperiments (scripts in scratchpad/review/sk3_trim/, run on gsv39 with PYTHONPATH=repo). I used real speech from raw.githubusercontent: 16 LJSpeech sentences, AISHELL and FunASR Mandarin clips, 2 LibriSpeech clips, and jfk. Each was cleaned to imitate a vocoder: everything near the noise floor was replaced with -80 dBFS noise, the clip was cropped tightly at speech onset and offset, and 0.3 s of exact zeros was appended.\n- **Whole sentences (exp5):** the threshold stayed at p95-29.9 dB (median), with 0 ms of speech above p95-30 lost and a voiced ratio of 1.000.\n- **Phrases with no internal pause (exp5):** at most 30 ms of frames between p95-30 and p95-25 lost, and nothing above p95-20. Voiced ratio p5 was 0.935, median 1.000.\n- **Exact-zero tail versus -80 dB noise tail (exp4):** nothing is lost with the noise tail.\n- **Deliberate worst case (exp7):** every non-speech region forced to exact digital zero, which a VITS vocoder doesn't produce. The threshold rose by 2.5 dB at the median. On unmodified speech the extra cut was mostly 0-20 ms of low-level decay, with a few cases up to 85 ms. It only reached 150-230 ms when I also cut the last 250 ms by 12-18 dB by hand. The median voiced ratio was 0.996.\n\nEffect on the downstream claims:\n- select.py changes speed only when the median ratio is more than 3% away from 1, so the claimed slowdown of every narration doesn't happen.\n- `in_normal_range` already allows p10*0.92 to p90*1.08, so the claimed ~20% rate bias doesn't appear with real speech. The 1.69 s to 1.41 s drop came from the dummy worker's synthetic speech.\n\nThe proposed fix also has a cost. The `db > -100` filter is deliberate: it stops zero padding on a noisy real clip from pulling the noise estimate down to -100 dB and turning off the `noise + 6` guard. `trim_edges` is also applied to real clips (workflows.py:748), so counting zeros as the floor would make those noisy clips trim worse.\n\nOverall it is a theoretical edge case, not a defect that matters for this release."
      },
