R5f asks to \"rank the remaining by a combined score where similarity dominates (CER/rate/pitch as tie-breakers/penalties)\". For versions it asks for \"the overall percentage (duration-weighted mean of sentence percentages) and the ranking\". The code does exactly that: `_version_score` (engine.py:894-914) is the duration-weighted pct plus the scorer's rate/pitch terms, which R5e names explicitly. When two candidates or versions both show 100%, the rate, pitch and CER terms decide. That is the tie-break role the spec gives them, and the ranking stays consistent with the percentages the user sees.\n\n2) The cap is a sensible choice. pct = 100 means \"as similar as your own real recordings typically are\". Above p50_real, a candidate sits inside the spread of the user's own recordings (calibration stores p10/p50/p90, and real clips themselves range up to p90). TTS output beating real recordings on speaker similarity is a known effect (it is more \"canonical\" than real recordings), so a higher raw cosine there does not reliably mean more like the person. Breaking ties in that range on F0-median/spread match to the user's profile and on rate match is reasonable. Pitch match is itself a voice-likeness cue, not noise. The select.py difference (uncapped s/p50 for checkpoint selection) is a separate, deliberate metric for picking a model. It does not show the per-candidate scorer is wrong.\n\n3) The cited \"real run\" is not real evidence. ws1/out/perfect.report.json has backend `dummy`, model `dummy-150`, similarity models `['mfcc-stats']` (marked unreliable, p50 = 0.9945, n = 3) and similarity_filter = False. Nearly every candidate saturates at 100 there because MFCC cannot tell voices apart, not because a fine-tuned GPT-SoVITS model does. Scenario X/Y (s = 1.15*p50) is hypothetical. With the real ensemble (ERes2NetV2 + resemblyzer, each capped separately, then averaged), full saturation needs both models above their own p50. A resemblyzer ratio of 1.15 is impossible when p50 is about 0.88.\n\n4) For A/B: both versions come from the same selected candidates, so if both average 99.7% they really are tied on the user's own metric. The recommendation then follows the secondary terms R5e asks for, and exact ties go to the unprocessed version (engine.py:941). The note prints both percentages and both scores openly. Both files are always saved, and the user picks the final one with `choose_variant`. Nothing is lost or misrepresented.\n\nAt most this is an optional refinement (break ties on uncapped s/p50). It is not a correctness defect, and it has no demonstrated impact with real models.\n---\nThe mechanics are as described, but this is a design choice that follows the spec, not a clear defect for this release.\n\n1. The mechanics check out. pct_from_sim (voicetwin/eval/speaker.py:387-391) returns 100*min(1, s/p50). Scorer.score (metrics.py:320) adds w_speaker*pct/100, and default_config.yaml:156 sets speaker to 2.0. _select (engine.py:523) and _version_score/_make_variants (engine.py:894-943) rank on that total. So once every ensemble model is at or above its p50, candidates tie on the speaker term and CER/rate/pitch decide.\n\n2. That is what the spec asks for. R5f defines the percentage with the cap: \"pct = 100 × min(1, s / p50_real)\", where 100% means \"as similar as your own real recordings\". It then asks to \"rank the remaining by a combined score where similarity dominates (CER/rate/pitch as tie-breakers/penalties)\". For the versions it asks for \"per version (A/B) the overall percentage (duration-weighted mean of sentence percentages) and the ranking\". R5f comes after R5e, so the calibrated percentage is the similarity measure, and using it for the A/B score fits both. When every candidate is at 100%, they are tied on the user-defined similarity, and breaking the tie with prosody (rate/pitch against the user's profile) and CER is exactly the stated tie-breaker. It does not stop similarity from dominating.\n\n3. The scores it ignores are not a reliable signal. Being above p50_real means closer to the centroid than the user's typical real clip, so the clip is already inside the spread of the user's own recordings (p50 to p90). A higher cosine there does not show the clip is more like the user; a TTS output close to the average can simply be over-smoothed. The perfect tier already counts 99% and up as done (early_pct/target_pct = 99, engine.py:529-538), which is the same view.\n\n4. Saturation is only partial with real encoders. The default ensemble is ERes2NetV2 plus resemblyzer, and pct is the mean of per-model capped values (speaker.py:589-604). The speaker term ties only when every model is at or above its own p50. Otherwise the uncapped model still separates candidates.\n\n5. The cited \"real run\" does not show real-world impact. In scratchpad/review/audio/ws1/out/perfect.report.json the similarity block says models=['mfcc-stats'], reliable=False, p50=0.9945, n=3. That is the simple MFCC fallback with a fake backend, where every cosine is about 0.99. Raw cosine would rank just as meaninglessly there.\n\n6. The A/B result is not harmful. If both versions are at about 100%, both are \"as like you as your real recordings\". The UI and log show both percentages honestly (for example 99.7% vs 99.7%, a 0.0% gap), the user picks by ear with the radio, and an exact tie falls back to the raw version. Recommending the denoised version when both are capped matches R5d's wish for no background noise.\n\nSwitching the ranking to the uncapped ratio might be a refinement, but the current behaviour follows the user's definitions and does not cause concrete harm.\n---\nThe mechanics are described correctly, but this is a design choice the spec asks for, not a defect.\n\nWhat the code does: `Scorer.score` (voicetwin/eval/metrics.py:320) adds `w[\"speaker\"] * pct/100`, with speaker weight 2.0 set in default_config.yaml:156. `pct` comes from `pct_from_sim` in voicetwin/eval/speaker.py:391, which returns `100*min(1, s/p50)`. So any candidate at or above p50 gets the same speaker term.\n\nWhy it is not a defect:\n1. R5f sets the similarity measure to exactly `pct = 100 x min(1, s/p50_real)`, where 100% means \"as similar as your own real recordings\". It then says to rank by a combined score \"where similarity dominates (CER/rate/pitch as tie-breakers/penalties)\". When every candidate is at 100%, they are tied on the spec's own similarity measure, and the spec says CER/rate/pitch should then decide. That is what `_select` does. It ranks by (cer_ok, total), and similarity dominates until the cap is reached.\n2. The cap matches what the score means. A raw cosine above p50_real already sits inside the spread of the user's own clips (half of their real clips score above p50). Being even closer to the centroid means \"more average\", not clearly \"more like you\". Preferring an s=1.15*p50 candidate with 6% rate deviation over an s=1.00*p50 candidate with 3% is a matter of taste. Nothing shows the uncapped choice sounds closer.\n3. For the A/B versions, R5f (which comes after R5e) says the version score is the duration-weighted mean of the sentence percentages, shown on the version cards with ⭐ 推荐. `_version_score` (engine.py:894) uses that measure plus rate/pitch \"as in the scorer\", as R5e asks. Below the cap, calibrated pct is a monotone rescaling of raw cosine, so the result can only differ from raw similarity when both versions are saturated. In that case both count as \"as like you as your own recordings\". Both files are always saved, and the user can switch with the radio and `choose_variant`.\n4. The \"real run\" numbers do not support the claim. I checked scratchpad/review/audio/ws1/out/perfect.report.json, and its per-segment `pcts`/`sims` show only `mfcc-stats` (raw cosine about 0.98–0.998), the unreliable fallback scorer, which saturates almost trivially. Nothing shows that ERes2NetV2 plus resemblyzer, on a real fine-tuned GPT-SoVITS model, often puts all candidates at 100% on every model. The ensemble pct averages capped values from each model, so it saturates only when every model is above its own p50 at once.\n5. select.py using the uncapped s/p50 is a different calibration choice, for picking a checkpoint across validation sentences. It does not show that the scorer's cap is a mistake.\n\nAt most this is a possible improvement to the tie-break (for example, add an uncapped term or report raw cosine for each version). It is not a major defect for this release."

When `dpo_user is None` and g >= DPO_AUTO_MIN_GB (22), clean material with few suspected typos gives `dpo = True`. default_config.yaml (`if_dpo: auto`), the CLI default `--dpo auto` and the WebUI radio default `auto` all lead into this path. The user never opts in. In the previous release (b518011) `if_dpo` was hard-coded False.\n\n2) This breaks the user's own spec chain:\n- R5 says 'Use only the research findings marked high or medium confidence'.\n- R5b says 'enable GPT DPO (if_dpo) when VRAM tier AND research support it'.\n- research_quality.md, the designated source, says no: §1.4 'DPO stays OFF by default on every tier (M)'. The §4 table for the high tier reads 'OFF by default. Opt-in in 高级设置 only… Auto-enable is not supported by H/M evidence'. §8.5 says 'Do not auto-enable DPO (M)'. The DPO verdict reads 'keep it opt-in'.\n- The deviation is deliberate. The U4 notes in v015_build.json say 'DPO deviates on purpose from research_quality.md ('never auto'), following R5b'. That reads R5b's condition backwards: 'when … research support it' is a precondition, and the research says it is not met.\n- The module docstring claims '没有测量依据的猜测一律不用'. The same docstring says DPO turns on automatically at ≥22 GB. The research says no source measures a quality gain from DPO, so the module contradicts itself.\n\n3) It matters in practice. Every user with a desktop 3090/4090/5090 gets an experimental GPT mode by default:\n- GPT training is 2-4x slower. Each step runs two forward passes at half the batch.\n- All GPT checkpoints are DPO-trained, so the automatic selection never compares against a non-DPO model.\n- The research reports that the 2025-06-04 v2pro integration package users install predates PR #2733. In that package the omission (`lost_P`) negatives never run, so DPO only learns against repetition. I could not check that PR directly (GitHub access is denied for that repo). The reference clone at 48b1a01 does have the fixed `randint(0,2)`, which fits the claim that it was a later fix.\n\nMitigations considered:\n- The WebUI has a 自动/开/关 radio and the CLI has `--dpo off`, so a user can turn it off. That does not make auto-on the right default for a non-technical user, and the research asks for the opposite (off, with an opt-in).\n- The VRAM side looks safe. GPT batch 4-6 at 22-24 GB matches the community DPO table, so out-of-memory is not the problem. The problem is the policy.\n\nRelevant files: <仓库>/voicetwin/backends/gptsovits.py (lines 15, 91, 323-345), <仓库>/voicetwin/default_config.yaml:75, <仓库>/voicetwin/cli.py:129-130, <仓库>/voicetwin/webui/app.py:125,2647.\n---\nI tried to refute this and could not. The function's own docstring says \"尽量让每个模型存 5 个左右（3~8 个…）\", but the code allows 2, so the code breaks its own stated contract.\n2. A teacher who types 22 in 音色训练轮数 always gets the auto interval, and the UI gives her no way to fix it (only config.yaml/CLI does).\n3. 22 is within SOVITS_MAX_EPOCHS=25, a plausible \"more is better\" choice. 26/34/37/38/46 are within GPT_MAX_EPOCHS=50.\n4. R5b explicitly asks to \"SAVE MORE CHECKPOINTS … so the automatic selection can choose the best epoch among more candidates\".\n\nLimits on severity:\n- The default auto path is unaffected. Epochs 8, 12 and 15 give 4, 6 and 5 checkpoints.\n- tests/test_gptsovits_fake.py:438 asserts `2 <= e // s <= 8`, so the author knowingly allowed 2. A fix will need that test bound updated (e.g. to `>= 3`).\n- The suggested fix is cheap and stays within the max: try epochs+1 and +2 when the best count is under 3. That gives 22 -> (24, 4) and 46 -> (48, 8).\n---\nThe arithmetic in the finding is correct, but the defect does not hold up as something that matters for this release.\n\nReproduced with gsv39 python, PYTHONPATH=repo: _auto_save_every gives 22->(22,11), 26->(26,13), 34->(34,17), 37->(38,19), 38->(38,19) and 46->(46,23). Each of these saves only 2 checkpoints. GPT-SoVITS really does export only at multiples of the save interval (ref s2_train.py:514, s1_train.py:50), so in these cases selection sees 2 candidates.\n\nWhy this is not a release-worthy defect:\n1. The automatic path that R5b is about never reaches this case. plan_training (voicetwin/backends/gptsovits.py:298-308) picks SoVITS 8 or 12 epochs and GPT 15 epochs. Those give (8,2)=4, (12,2)=6 and (15,3)=5 checkpoints, which is exactly the \"save more checkpoints\" behaviour R5b asks for. The same holds for every other auto-chosen epoch count.\n2. Hitting it takes a manual override in 高级设置: 音色训练轮数 (webui/app.py:2642, default 0 = 自动) set to exactly 22, or GPT set to 22/26/34/37/38/46. The target user is a non-technical teacher whom the requirements steer toward fully automatic settings, so this is a narrow edge case.\n3. Allowing 2 checkpoints is the intended design, enforced by a test. tests/test_gptsovits_fake.py:436 asserts `2 <= e // s <= 8 and e in (epochs, epochs + 1)` for every epoch count from 3 to 50. The function deliberately never moves the user's chosen epoch count by more than +1. The comment at line 191 confirms that an even count can always fall back to 2. The suggested fix changes the user's explicit 22 to 24, which contradicts that design and the respect for explicit overrides.\n4. Even in the edge case, selection still compares the final epoch and the midpoint (e11 and e22), and the plan summary says plainly \"每 11 轮存一次模型\". Nothing breaks and the final weights are not lost.\n\nThe only real gap is in the wording. The docstring says \"3~8 个\" and the default_config.yaml comment says \"5 个左右\", which does not match the 2-checkpoint result for these few manual inputs. That is cosmetic and minor, not a functional defect for v0.1.6."

The doctor (workflows.py:982) marks noisereduce as optional and offers no way to install it.\n\n4. **It breaks a stated requirement:** R5e (v015_requirements.md:176-184) asks for both 未去杂音 and 去杂音 versions in the 完美 tier, with the UI showing two players and a recommendation. Skipping B gracefully is meant only for the case where noisereduce is not installed. The recommended install, which is what the teacher uses, never installs it, so the headline feature of the perfect tier never runs for her. The tests (test_pipeline.py:279) cover the fallback but not the installer.\n\nThe suggested fix is small and low risk. In gsv mode, add `noisereduce`, for example with `--no-deps`, because its dependencies (numpy, scipy, librosa, tqdm, matplotlib) normally come with the integration package.         "evidence": "I could not refute this one. It is a real defect, though minor.\n\n1) How the engine picks: voicetwin/synth/engine.py:944 chooses the recommended version with `max(variants, key=lambda v: (v[\"score\"], ...))`. `score` comes from `_version_score`. It is the duration-weighted mean of Scorer.score().total, which is pct/100 minus 0.6*rate_dev, minus 0.3*pitch_dev, minus clip and other penalties (voicetwin/eval/metrics.py:303-380). The per-version `pct` is a separate weighted mean of pct alone. The two can rank A and B differently.\n\n2) The flip can happen in practice. Version B is the denoised copy of the same audio, so the two pct values are usually very close (pct is also capped at 100). Denoising lowers the noise floor. That changes auto_silence_threshold and therefore the voiced duration in speech_activity (voicetwin/utils/audio.py:182-197), which changes rate_dev, and f0 stats can shift too. A voiced-time change of about 2% moves the rate penalty by about 0.012. That outweighs a pct gap of 0.9 points (0.009). So the version with the lower pct winning on score is a normal outcome.\n\n3) What the UI says: voicetwin/webui/app.py:1131-1135 computes `gap = f\"（高 {abs(pr - po):.1f} 个百分点）\"` whenever |pr-po| >= 0.05. It never checks that pr > po. The engine's own log line (engine.py:950-952) uses a signed gap, but the UI uses abs() and the word 高.\n\n4) Reproduced with the py3.9 venv (PYTHONPATH=repo, script at scratchpad/review/vmd_check.py), using A{pct 98.2, score 0.95, recommended} and B{pct 99.1, score 0.93}. The impact is cosmetic but visible to the user, and it contradicts the cards on the same screen. The defect is real and reachable.\n\n1) Selection and display use different metrics. Nothing makes the version with the higher score also have the higher pct.\n\n2) The UI hides the sign. I checked with scratchpad/review/s2_pen.py, using the real `denoise_light` and `Scorer` on synthetic voiced clips at three noise levels. Denoising changes the non-pct part of each sentence's total (pitch_dev, and sometimes rate) by about ±0.001-0.004, in either direction. That equals ±0.1-0.4 percentage points. Denoising generated speech usually moves A-vs-B pct only a little, and pct is rounded to 0.1. Near the 99-100% range the user is aiming for, the 100% cap shrinks the gap further. So a version with the higher score but a lower (or rounded-lower) pct is plausible in real perfect-tier runs.         "evidence": "I could not refute this. The bug is real and is new in this release (_denoised does not exist at b518011). It does not use the force/redo flag. So a forced redo keeps the same `plan.wav_path`.\n- `synthesize_segment(force=True)` (l.561-568) only adds a random offset to seed0. At l.687 it writes the new best take over that same `plan.wav_path` with `save_audio(plan.wav_path, best.wav, best.sr)`. Nothing deletes `<key>.dn{sr}.wav`.\n- `_denoised()` (l.960-977) returns the cached `.dn` file whenever `s == sr and len(w) == len(wav)`. Sample count is the only check. No other code in the repo removes `.dn` files; the only other mention is at l.1105, which just excludes them from a count.\n- Redo is a feature users are pushed toward. The WebUI has the \"只重新生成第几句\" textbox (app.py:2677). The CLI has `--redo` and prints a \"想只重做这几句：加上 --redo …\" hint (cli.py:619), and the summary says \"可以只重做这几句\". I saved an \"old take\" to the cache path and called `Narrator._denoised`. Then I overwrote the path with a different \"new take\" of the same length and called it again. The second call returned exactly the old take's denoised audio: allclose with the first result was True, correlation with the new take was -0.0006 and with the old take was 0.9995. So version B silently keeps the old take.\n\nHow likely: `trim_edges` cuts on a 5 ms grid (hop 160 samples at 32 kHz). GPT-SoVITS appends 0.3 s of silence after each fragment (TTS.py l.1550-1560), so the end is not clamped to the raw length. The trimmed length is therefore the voiced span rounded to 5 ms. Re-takes of the same sentence vary in length by roughly 0.1-0.3 s, and the 完美 tier keeps takes within the normal speaking-rate range, which narrows that further. My estimate is about 0.5-2% chance per redone sentence. That is rare but not just theoretical, and teachers will redo several flagged sentences over many narrations.\n\nImpact: the take the teacher asked to replace stays in version B. Version B is then scored on that old take and may be recommended, which puts the old take in the final `<stem>.wav`. Meanwhile the results table shows the new take's score. The fix is about one line: delete `<stem>.dn*.wav` when a new take is saved, or validate the cache by content hash or seed instead of length. The finding's \"minor\" severity looks right.\n---\nI tried to refute this and could not. The bug is real and I reproduced it.\n\nCode path (HEAD 83bff05):\n- `synthesize_segment(force=True)` (engine.py:561-695) is what both CLI `--redo` (cli.py:519) and the web UI's 只重新生成第几句 box (app.py:2156 -> workflows.py:561 -> `narrate(redo=...)`) end up calling. It changes only the seed (`seed0 += random.randint(...)`). The cache key from `_plan` does not include the seed, so `plan.wav_path` stays the same.\n- It overwrites that file with `save_audio(plan.wav_path, best.wav, best.sr)` at line 687. No code anywhere in the repo deletes or invalidates `<key>.dn{sr}.wav`: the only `.dn` mentions are engine.py:962 and the clip counter at :1105.\n- `_denoised` (engine.py:960-978) reuses the cache whenever `s == sr and len(w) == len(wav)`. The audio content is never checked.\n- The trimmed length is quantised. In `trim_edges` (engine.py:154-183), length = (v_last - v_first) * hop + 2*half + 2*pad, where hop = 5 ms (160 samples at 32 kHz). So a take's length only lands on a 5 ms grid.\n\nExperiment: scratchpad/review/sk2_dn_stale.py, run with the gsv39 venv and real noisereduce.\n- I made two different takes, a 150 Hz tone and a 220 Hz tone, with different noise. Both trimmed to 66880 samples.\n- I called `Narrator._denoised` on the old take, which wrote k.dn32000.wav. Then I called it on the new take with the same path.\n- The result had corr = 1.000 with the old take and 0.000 with the new one. So version B silently holds the old take.\n- Sweeping the duration across a 200 ms span gave only 41 distinct trimmed lengths, in steps of exactly 160 samples.\n\nImpact:\n- R5e says version B must be \"the same selected candidates, each lightly denoised\". After a redo whose new take happens to match the old length, B holds the old (often flagged) take. B is scored with use_asr=False, so a misread word is not caught.\n- If B is recommended, or the teacher picks it, `<stem>.wav` ships the old sentence while the report shows the new seed and %.\n- Timing stays consistent because the lengths are equal, so nothing looks wrong and nothing warns.\n\nOdds: with GPT-SoVITS take lengths spread by roughly 0.1-0.2 s for one sentence, a collision on the 5 ms grid happens about 1-2% of the time per redone sentence. That is rare, but it hits the headline perfect-tier \"redo the flagged sentences\" workflow and fails silently.\n\nFix: when a new best take is saved, delete the `.dn*.wav` files next to it, or key the dn cache on the seed or a hash of the audio. Severity minor, but a genuine defect.\n---\nI tried to refute this and couldn't. The bug is real, and the code that causes it is new in this release.\n\nHow it happens:\n- `_plan()` (engine.py:470-479) builds the cache key from text, model, refs, speed, tier and base_seed. It does not include the random seed offset that a forced redo adds (engine.py:568-569). So `synthesize_segment(force=True)` overwrites the same `plan.wav_path` (engine.py:687).\n- Nothing deletes `<key>.dn{sr}.wav`. The only references to `.dn` in the whole package are engine.py:962 (the cache itself) and engine.py:1105 (`clear_cache`, which counts files and then deletes the whole folder).\n- `_denoised()` (engine.py:960-978) reuses the cached file whenever `s == sr and len(w) == len(wav)`. Its only staleness check is the sample count.\n- `narrate()` passes `redo` to `synthesize_all` and then calls `_make_variants` on the same results. The web UI's redo goes through the same path (workflows.py:561). So this affects the Perfect tier (`variants: True`, engine.py:103).\n\nExperiment (scratchpad/review/dn_stale.py, gsv39 venv, PYTHONPATH=repo, noisereduce available): I wrote take A at a cache path and ran `Narrator._denoised`, which created `abcd.dn32000.wav`. I then saved a different take of the same length at the same path, as a redo does, and called `_denoised` again. It returned the old version B: `np.allclose(b1, b2)` was True, its correlation with the new take was about 0.00 and with the old take 1.0.\n\nHow likely equal lengths are (scratchpad/review/dn_collide.py): `trim_edges` cuts on a 5 ms grid (all length differences were multiples of 160 samples). GPT-SoVITS output length comes in 1280-sample token steps, and the backend appends `fragment_interval` 0.3 s, so the end is never clamped. With a rough GPT-SoVITS-like signal, two takes of a 3 s sentence matched in length 3-6% of the time (duration sigma 0.15 s and 0.08 s). Real speech is noisier, so the true rate is probably lower, but it is clearly more than theoretical.\n\nWhat softens it:\n- It only happens on a length match, and only matters when version B is recommended.\n- If the old take was flagged for low similarity, B scores lower and A tends to win.\n- If it was flagged for misread words, `_version_score` runs with `use_asr=False`, so B can still win with the bad sentence.\n- Redoing again almost always clears it.\n\nWhat the user sees: a sentence she explicitly asked to redo silently comes back as the old take in the final `<stem>.wav`, while the table and the single-sentence clip show the new take.\n\nFix: delete `plan.wav_path.with_name(plan.wav_path.stem + '.dn*.wav')` (glob) after `save_audio` at engine.py:687, or include the seed or a content hash in the dn cache name. Severity is minor, but it is a genuine defect worth fixing in this release."

That means two takes have equal length only when their voiced spans match to the 5 ms frame (160 samples at 32 kHz). GPT-SoVITS takes of one sentence usually vary by a few hundred ms in duration, so I estimate about 0.5-2% per redone sentence. It isn't hypothetical, though. The flow invites redos: the summary says \"可以只重做这几句\" and the CLI prints \"--redo ...\" for flagged sentences. So over many redos it will eventually happen.\n\nWhat happens when it does: version B silently keeps the rejected take, for example one with a misread word. B is also scored on that old audio, so the A/B recommendation is based on wrong data. If B is recommended or chosen, `<stem>.wav` undoes the redo for that sentence. Timing and subtitles still line up because the lengths match, so nothing looks wrong.\n\nThe fix is cheap: delete `<key>.dn*.wav` in `synthesize_segment` right after `save_audio`, or put the seed or a content hash in the denoise cache name.\n\nVerdict: a real correctness bug, minor severity, low probability.\n---\nI tried to refute this and couldn't. It never compares the content.\n- `narrate` (L781 → L794) calls `synthesize_all(segments, redo)` and then `_make_variants`, so in the same run the new take is denoised against the old cache file. It stays stale until a later redo happens to produce a different length.\n\nHow likely a length match is:\n- `trim_edges` cuts on a 5 ms grid. On simulated takes of one 3 s sentence (duration σ = 0.25 s), the minimum step between lengths was exactly 160 samples.\n- In that simulation two independent takes had the same length about 0.6% of the time. Short sentences, or takes that vary less, push this toward 1-3%.\n\nWho it affects:\n- Only the 完美 tier, since `\"variants\": True` is set only in `QUALITY_PRESETS[\"perfect\"]`.\n- It changes the final file only when version B is recommended or chosen.\n\nWhat goes wrong when it fires:\n- Version B silently contains the rejected old take for that sentence. Requirement R-variants says B must be \"the same selected candidates, each lightly denoised\", so this breaks it.\n- The per-sentence table shows the new take's scores, so the report doesn't match the audio.\n- If B is the final version, the teacher's redo is undone without any sign.\n\nSo it is not theoretical, just infrequent. Adopting one row silently undoes hand edits on other rows. For a non-technical teacher working through 200+ clips, this is quiet data loss that is unlikely to be noticed, and it lowers transcript quality, which feeds training. The 「改完一定要点保存修改」 instruction does not prepare users for this. The cause: `on_voice_change` takes no backend input; its inputs are only `[voice, only_sus]`.\n\nImpact: for a non-technical teacher, the normal ①→②→③ flow tells her to redo a step she has just finished, which directly undermines the guidance this feature was built to give. Reloading the page clears it, but she would not know to do that.\n\nThe fix is cheap: add `gen_warn` to the `after_task` outputs or to `PREP_OUT`/`TRAIN_OUT`, and/or add a ③ tab `.select(lambda v, b: _gen_warn_md(cfg, v, b), [voice, s_backend], gen_warn)`. Also add `s_backend` as an input to the voice-change handler. Severity \"major\" is arguable since it is a stale message and nothing is lost, but it is a genuine defect worth fixing for this release."

The button then says \"click once more within 5 seconds\" for the rest of the run. If the teacher later clicks once as the label tells her, nothing happens.\n- **Scope:** this is new code in this release. Its docstring says this was done on purpose.\n- **No reset anywhere else:** the button is wired only as `c[name].click(self.on_stop, armed, [c[name], armed], show_progress=\"hidden\")` (app.py:2536), with no `.then` and no timer. The label is unchanged and there is no progress indicator because of `show_progress=\"hidden\"`. A non-technical teacher cannot tell that her click was ignored and the task is still running.\n\nThe code is new in this release: the diff vs b518011 adds STOP_CONFIRM, `_StopOnce` and `on_stop`. The fix is cheap (a different label when re-arming, or resetting after STOP_WINDOW). It is a real, minor UX defect, not a theoretical one.\n---\nThe mechanics are as described, but I don't think this is a defect worth fixing for this release.\n\nWhat I confirmed:\n- `on_stop` is in <仓库>/voicetwin/webui/app.py, lines 1772-1783. When the earlier click is older than STOP_WINDOW (5 s), it returns `_upd(value=STOP_CONFIRM), now`. The label is the same as after the first click.\n- `_StopOnce` (lines 1588-1601) returns an empty update after the first stream tick, so nothing else sets the label back.\n- `page_js` has no script that resets it either.\n- I ran `on_stop` in the py3.9/gradio-4.24 venv. A click 8 s late gave the same STOP_CONFIRM label and did not call `request_stop`. The next click within 5 s gave STOP_PENDING and called `request_stop` once.\n\nWhy I still say real=false:\n1) It fails safe and fixes itself. A late click never stops anything by mistake, and the label stays on 「再点一次确认停止」. The obvious next move is to click again, and that click stops the task, because the late click re-armed the 5 s window. The finding's own scenario admits the worst case is one extra click.\n2) The label states the rule: 「（5 秒内）」. She sees no wrong status. Until a real stop she never sees 「正在停止……」 or 「⏹ 已停止」, the progress bar keeps moving, and if she tries to start another task the page tells her one is still running and to click ⏹ 停止. So she cannot wrongly believe the task has stopped.\n3) This is deliberate, tested behaviour. tests/test_webui_helpers.py:511 asserts that a late click re-arms with STOP_CONFIRM (「太久以前点的第一次：重新确认」). The sticky label was chosen after a browser test where the confirm text disappeared too fast (see the `_StopOnce` docstring).\n4) No requirement covers it. v015_requirements.md only specifies the grey ⏹ 已停止 bar. The audit's design (ux_audit.json) only says the first click changes the label and a second click within the window stops. It says nothing about changing the label back when the window expires.\n\nThe fix is cheap, but this is a small wording polish item, not a defect that matters for v0.1.6."

The device is \"cuda\" when CUDA is available (similarity.device defaults to \"auto\" → _torch_device).\n\n2. **Memory grows in step with clip length.** I ran the real GPT-SoVITS ref/GPT_SoVITS/eres2net/ERes2NetV2.py (baseWidth=24, scale=4, expansion=4) on CPU, using torch 2.14 under no_grad (script: scratchpad/review/eres_mem.py). Peak extra RSS:\n   - 10 s: 0.39 GB\n   - 30 s: 0.95 GB\n   - 60 s: 1.89 GB\n   \n   That is about 32 MB per second of audio. By extrapolation a 5-minute file needs about 9.6 GB, 10 minutes about 19 GB and 15 minutes about 28 GB. The architecture explains why: layer1 has stride 1 and 256 channels over the full 80×T grid, and bn3, conv3 and the shortcut tensors are alive at the same time. The requirements' example GPU is an RTX 5070 with 12 GB, and high/mid VRAM defaults to the perfect tier. A lecture of a few minutes or more therefore runs out of memory. Those rows show an empty ERes2NetV2 column, and their 综合 % comes from resemblyzer alone. Resemblyzer splits its input into 1.6 s windows itself, so it copes with long files. The sentence clips, meanwhile, use the two-model average, so the ranking compares unlike numbers.\n\n5. **Leftover VRAM can hurt the next generation.** GPT-SoVITS inference runs in a separate api_v2.py subprocess (backends/gptsovits.py:1186). After a large allocation or an OOM, PyTorch's caching allocator in the web UI process keeps that VRAM reserved. The api_v2 process cannot use it, which can slow down the next generation or make it fail.\n\n6. **This is new in this release.** ERes2NetV2Encoder and verify_files are not in b518011.\n\nThe real GPT-SoVITS only runs this model on short reference clips, so the long-input case is a new problem the review should keep. The fix is to split the audio into windows of about 10 s, embed each window and average the L2-normalised results, then call empty_cache.\n---\nI tried to refute this and could not. The defect is real.\n\n1) Code path, confirmed at HEAD 83bff05. `ERes2NetV2Encoder.embed` (voicetwin/eval/speaker.py:198-211) resamples the audio and trims only leading and trailing silence (`trim_silence` cuts head and tail, nothing in the middle). It then runs a single `kaldi.fbank` over the whole clip and a single `self._model(feat.unsqueeze(0).to(self._device))`. Nothing chunks or caps the length: `load_audio` (utils/audio.py:35) does not truncate either. `SimilarityJudge.embed` (speaker.py:605-612) catches every exception and logs it at debug level only, so `judge_embeddings` averages whatever models succeeded.\n\n2) Full-length files are in the default list. In the UI, the 开始鉴别 handler (webui/app.py:2369-2381) fills an empty selection with `_report_audio_files` (app.py:1366). That list holds every variant path (the full `未去杂音` / `去杂音` renders) and, when there are no variants, the final full `report[\"audio\"]`. So the whole narration is scored in one pass with the default settings, on every quality tier, not only 完美. `wf.verify_defaults` (workflows.py:832-853) does the same. `verify_files` calls `judge.judge_file(p)` on each file (workflows.py:884).\n\n3) Memory measurement. I ran the real GPT-SoVITS ERes2NetV2 (`baseWidth=24, scale=4, expansion=4`, random weights, `no_grad`, CPU, torch 2.14) using review/eres_mem.py. Extra peak memory was 0.39 GB at 10 s, 0.95 GB at 30 s and 1.89 GB at 60 s. That is linear at about 32 MB per second of audio, so roughly 9.6 GB at 5 min, 19 GB at 10 min and 29 GB at 15 min. It matches the claimed layer1 activation of 256 ch x 80 x T x 4 bytes, with several such tensors live inside each bottleneck block.\n\n4) Process context. `similarity.device: auto` resolves to cuda in the web UI process. GPT-SoVITS inference runs in a separate api_v2.py subprocess (backends/gptsovits.py), which already holds VRAM. On a typical 8-12 GB card, any narration longer than about 2-5 minutes will either:\n- throw CUDA OOM, which is swallowed, leaving an empty ERes2NetV2 column and a 综合 % from resemblyzer alone while the sentence clips get the two-model average; or\n- on recent Windows NVIDIA drivers with the default sysmem fallback, spill into shared memory and run extremely slowly, failing anyway at 10+ minutes.\n\nAfter the OOM, the caching allocator in the web UI process keeps the blocks it reserved and never calls `empty_cache`. That VRAM is unavailable to the separate api_v2 process for the next generation. With no GPU, the CPU path tries to allocate about 19-29 GB of RAM for a 10-15 minute lecture.\n\n5) Nothing handles this elsewhere. The engine's own variant scoring (`synth/engine.py` `_version_score`) scores each sentence separately, but ⑤ 鉴别 (`verify_files`) scores whole files. resemblyzer is not affected, because `embed_utterance` works on 1.6 s partials, so only the ERes2NetV2 member breaks.\n\nThe impact is real for this release: the default 鉴别 flow, a new feature here, used on lecture-length output, fails silently and holds VRAM afterwards. The suggested fix is sound: embed about 10 s windows and use the L2-normalised mean embedding, and optionally call `torch.cuda.empty_cache()`.\n---\nI could not refute this. The code path is real, it runs by default, and nothing else in the code handles it.\n\n1. The memory estimate holds. In the real ERes2NetV2 (ref/GPT-SoVITS/GPT_SoVITS/eres2net/ERes2NetV2.py), `layer1 = _make_layer(block, m_channels=64, ..., stride=1)` with expansion=4 gives 256 output channels at full 80×T resolution. In the first block, these are alive at the same time:\n   - x (64 channels)\n   - spx/bn1 output (96)\n   - conv3 output and bn3 output (256 + 256)\n   - shortcut conv and bn outputs (256 + 256)\n   \n   That is roughly 600 to 950 channels × 80 × T × 4 bytes. It works out to about 1.2-1.8 GB per minute of audio:\n   - 10 minutes: about 12-18 GB\n   - 5 minutes: about 6-9 GB\n   \n   That is beyond a 12 GB card (the RTX 5070 used as the example in R2). R8 itself says the default is \"the latest narration's versions/variants\". `verify_files` then calls `judge.judge_file(p)` on each file (workflows.py:884).\n\n4. The failure is hidden. `SimilarityJudge.embed` (speaker.py:605-611) catches every Exception with `log.debug`. A CUDA OOM, a CPU \"not enough memory\" error or a cuDNN failure just drops \"eres2netv2\" for that row. `judge_embeddings` then averages only the members that are left (speaker.py:601), so 综合 % for the full versions is resemblyzer alone, and their ERes2NetV2 cell is blank with no explanation. The sentence clips in the same ranked table use the two-model average.\n\n5. Nothing mitigates it. The only `torch.cuda.empty_cache` in the codebase is in data/proofcheck.py. `similarity.device` defaults to `auto`, which means cuda in the web UI process. Two consequences:\n   - When a long forward pass succeeds, PyTorch's caching allocator keeps many GB reserved inside the web UI process. The separate GPT-SoVITS worker can't use that VRAM for the next generation.\n   - On Windows, the sysmem-fallback / WDDM paging may make the pass extremely slow instead of failing.\n\n6. The only other callers of the judge (metrics.py scorer, synth/select.py) score one sentence at a time. So the default ⑤ 鉴别 run is the one path that feeds multi-minute files into ERes2NetV2.\n\nI could not run it: no torch wheel is available in this sandbox (the CPU index is blocked by the proxy, and the PyPI wheel needs CUDA libraries). It matters for this release: R5f/R8 ask for accurate per-model columns and a fair ranking. The fix the finding proposes (embed about 10 s windows and use the L2-normalised mean, then empty the CUDA cache) is the right one."

The diff against b518011 shows this line was added in this release; the old output naming in workflows.py:512 used ASCII `'%Y%m%d_%H%M%S'`. The only caller is `do_generate` at app.py:2165. It is not inside any try block and runs before the first progress yield, so an exception aborts every click on 生成 and Gradio shows a generic error.\n\n2. **CPython 3.9 really takes this path on Windows.** In Modules/timemodule.c (3.9), lines 684-686 read `#ifdef MS_WINDOWS /* wcsftime() doesn't format correctly time zones, see issue #10653 */ #undef HAVE_WCSFTIME`. So `time_strftime` runs `format = PyUnicode_EncodeLocale(format_arg, \"surrogateescape\")` (line 780). UTF-8 mode is ignored on that path, so the `PYTHONUTF8` set in worker.py would not help even if the web UI used it. Nothing in the repo, GPT-SoVITS or gradio changes it later. matplotlib only calls setlocale when `axes.formatter.use_locale` is on, which is off by default. The launcher's `chcp 65001` only changes the console code page, not the C-runtime locale.\n\n4. **Correction to the scenario:** `setlocale(LC_CTYPE, \"\")` uses the user's Regional format, not the system ANSI code page (\"language for non-Unicode programs\") as the finding says. I checked which code pages can encode 月, 日, 点 and 分:\n   - **Works:** cp936 (Simplified Chinese, China) and cp932 (Japanese) encode all four.\n   - **Fails:** cp1252 (English or Western European) fails on all four. cp950 (Traditional Chinese, Taiwan or Hong Kong) fails on 点.\n\n**Who is affected:** a default mainland-China Windows install works. In fileutils.c encode_locale_ex (lines 777-789), that goes straight to encode_current_locale (wcstombs under the CRT LC_CTYPE). UTF-8 mode and PYTHONUTF8 are ignored on this path, so `PYTHONUTF8=1` in worker.py would not help. It is only set for the worker subprocess anyway.\n- preconfig.c calls `_Py_SetLocaleFromEnv(LC_CTYPE)` at startup, which is setlocale(LC_CTYPE, \"\") and uses the system ANSI code page.\n- surrogateescape does not cover a real CJK character, so the call raises UnicodeEncodeError.\n\n2) Which code pages fail (tested with Python's codecs):\n- '%m月%d日%H点%M分' encodes fine in cp936, cp932, cp949 and utf-8.\n- It fails in cp1252 (on '月').\n- It also fails in cp950, the Traditional Chinese code page for Taiwan and Hong Kong, on the simplified '点' (U+70B9).\n\n3) Reachability: `_output_path` (app.py:1181-1186) is called in do_generate (app.py:2165) before any yield and with no try/except. The pattern arrived in d22b52d. workflows.py:512 still uses the ASCII-safe '%Y%m%d_%H%M%S'.\n\n5) Why the impact is limited (the reason this is minor):\n- The target user is a mainland teacher: the app points to nvidia.cn, and config.py/project.py treat Notepad \"ANSI\" as GBK.\n- Mainland Windows has ACP 936, where the format encodes fine. I checked it two ways: by reading the code and by running it on the real engine.\n\nCode (voicetwin/synth/engine.py at 83bff05):\n- Line 625: the adaptive loop stops only when `self._meets_targets(self._select(cands, seg.lang)[0], seg)` is true.\n- `_select` (lines 519-525) returns `max(pool, key=(_cer_ok, total))`, so only the top-by-total candidate is ever tested against the targets.\n- After the loop, the same top-by-total candidate is saved.\n- Line 668 then adds '这一句可能不够像，建议重新生成或改写' whenever `met` is False and no other hint was added. That covers a miss on CER target, pct ≥ 99, or rate/pitch range.\n\nWhy this happens in practice:\n- `total` trades similarity against other penalties. With the default weights in default_config.yaml (speaker 2.0, cer 1.0, rate 0.4, pitch 0.2), 1 point of pct is worth only 0.02 of total. That is the same as about a 5% rate deviation (rate_dev 0.05 × 0.4).\n- Example: a candidate at 98.8% with perfect rate (fails the 99% target) outranks one at 99.3% with a 10% rate deviation. The second one is still well inside `in_normal_range` (p10×0.92 to p90×1.08) and meets every target.\n- The finding's numbers (totals 1.958 and 1.960) fit these weights.\n\nExperiment (scratchpad/review/adaptive_top/test_exp.py, gsv39 venv, PYTHONPATH=repo):\n- Setup: real `run_narrate` with quality=perfect. `Scorer.score` was patched so the 1st candidate is pct 99.3, CER 0, total 1.946, and every later one is pct 98.9, CER 0, total 1.978. `in_normal_range` was patched to return True.\n- Result: tries 20, met False, chosen pct 98.9, flagged True, hint '这一句可能不够像，建议重新生成或改写', status ⚠️. The log said '平均每句试了 20.0 次；0 句达到了「完美」的严格标准'.\n- So a candidate that met every target on the very first try was ignored, 16 extra generations were spent, and a candidate that misses the target was kept and wrongly flagged.\n\nRequirement it breaks: R5c says 'stop early only when a candidate meets strict targets ... If no candidate meets targets, keep the best-scoring one and flag'. The code flags a sentence even when some candidate met the targets, and it does not stop early in that case.\n\nImpact: perfect is the default tier for mid/high VRAM. The bug causes up to 5× generation time per affected sentence (20 tries instead of 4), a false ⚠️ flag in the results table, and a wrong 'met' count in the summary. It shows up mostly when candidate pct values sit around the 99% target. The hint text also blames similarity when the actual miss was CER or rate/pitch.\n\nThe existing tests (test_perfect_adaptive_stops_when_targets_met, test_perfect_adaptive_hard_cap_and_flag) only use uniform pct sequences, so they never exercise this case. Severity 'minor' is appropriate, but it is a real logic defect.\n---\nI could not refute the main claim. In voicetwin/synth/engine.py at lines 625-627, the adaptive loop sets met=True only when `self._meets_targets(self._select(cands, seg.lang)[0], seg)` is true. `_select` (line 523) ranks by `(_cer_ok, total)`. `total` comes from Scorer.score in voicetwin/eval/metrics.py: `pct/100 - 0.6*|log(rate/p50)| - 0.3*pitch_dev - 2*cer`. `_meets_targets` checks something different: pct >= 99, CER <= 0.05 (or errors == 0), and rate/pitch inside p10*0.92..p90*1.08. A 1-point gap in pct is worth only 0.01 of `total`, while ordinary sampling spread in rate can cost 0.05-0.1. So the top candidate by total is often not the one that meets the targets.\n\nExperiment (scratchpad/review/s2_adaptive_met.py) runs the real Narrator._select, _meets_targets and _cer_ok and Scorer.in_normal_range on a stub, with rate profile p10/p50/p90 = 3.6/4.5/5.4:\n- X: 99.5%, CER 0, rate 5.3. total 0.897, meets=True.\n- Y: 97%, CER 0, rate 4.5. total 0.970, meets=False.\n- Result: best=Y, so met=False.\n- The finding's own edge case also reproduces (X2 99.5% in range, total 0.8427; Y2 100% just out of range, total 0.8436), and Y2 is chosen.\n\nEffect: the loop keeps generating up to the cap (20, about 5x the time for that sentence). It then keeps the top-by-total candidate (here 97%), adds the hint '这一句可能不够像' and status ⚠️ (lines 668-675), even though a candidate meeting every strict target was in the first batch. This goes against R5c ('stop early only when a candidate meets strict targets ... If no candidate meets targets, keep the best-scoring one and flag') and R5f ('In perfect, keep trying ... aiming for >= 99% before stopping early'). Nothing else in the code catches this, since `met` is only computed in the loop. test_perfect_adaptive_stops_when_targets_met patches in_normal_range to always True and gives all candidates the same rate and CER, so it never covers this case.\n\nOne part of the finding is weak: the complaint about the hint wording. R5c itself prescribes the text '这一句可能不够像，建议重新生成或改写' for any target miss, so the wording alone is not a defect. Severity minor is fair: there is no crash or bad audio, but it wastes time, picks a worse match than it already had, and flags the sentence when it should not, in the default high/mid-VRAM tier.\n---\nI could not refute the main claim. One part of it is wrong.\n\nCode, voicetwin/synth/engine.py: the 完美 loop exits only if `self._meets_targets(self._select(cands, seg.lang)[0], seg)` is true (lines ~625-627). `_select` takes the max by `(self._cer_ok(...), c.score.total)` and never looks at the targets. Nothing downstream recomputes `met`: the summary at line 873 counts `r.met`.\n\nWhy top-by-total and \"meets the targets\" often disagree: the shipped config weights in default_config.yaml are speaker 2.0, cer 1.0, rate 0.4, pitch 0.2. So 1 point of pct is worth 0.02 of total, and so is a rate_dev of 0.05 (about a 5% rate difference). Close to the 99% target (`target_pct: 99.0` in the perfect preset), small rate and pitch differences decide the ranking.\n\nExperiment: I ran scratchpad/review/adaptive_top/test_exp.py in the gsv39 venv against HEAD. It meets every target.\n- All later candidates: Y = 98.9%, total 1.978.\n- Result: `tries 20 met False pct 98.9 flagged True hint 这一句可能不够像… status ⚠️`. The log says \"0 句达到了「完美」的严格标准\". X was produced first and never chosen.\n\nHow often this happens, from a Monte Carlo with that scoring formula and 4-candidate batches up to 20 tries:\n- Setup: per-sentence pct about N(98, 1) capped at 100, rate variation 4-8%.\n- 6-13% of sentences end flagged ⚠️ even though one candidate met every target. That is most of the flags in this regime.\n- On average 1-2 extra tries per sentence.\n\nThis goes against R5c: \"stop early only when a candidate meets strict targets … If no candidate meets targets, keep the best-scoring one and flag\". The code also flags when another candidate did meet the targets, and it undercounts the per-narration \"met\" summary. Note that the `met` and flag outcome do describe the kept candidate correctly (98.9% < 99). The defect is that X, which met the targets, was ignored.\n\nWhat I refuted:\n- The hint wording is not a defect. R5c requires exactly \"这一句可能不够像，建议重新生成或改写\" whenever no kept candidate meets the targets, whichever target failed.\n- The finding's numbers are only illustrative. With the 1.958/1.960 totals as given, pct and rate alone cannot produce that ordering. The real scenario (X 99.3% with a slightly off rate, Y 98.9% with a perfect rate) is realistic and reproduced above.\n\nSeverity: minor. The effects are extra generation time and wrong ⚠️ flags or \"met\" counts on some sentences in the main new tier.\n\nFix: stop when any candidate meets the targets. In adaptive mode, make `_select` prefer target-meeting candidates, for example with the sort key `(cer_ok, meets_targets, total)`."

        "id": "auto-save-every-too-few-ckpts",
        "severity": "minor",
        "file": "voicetwin/backends/gptsovits.py",
        "line": 184,
        "claim": "`_auto_save_every` accepts only divisors that give 2-8 checkpoints and tries epochs+1 only when none exists. For 22 epochs the only valid divisor is 11, so it returns (22, 11): just 2 checkpoints. The same happens for 26/34/38/46 GPT epochs. This defeats R5b 'save more checkpoints so selection can choose among more'.",
        "scenario": "Teacher sets SoVITS 轮数 = 22 in 高级设置 and leaves save_every on auto. Only e11 and e22 are exported, so automatic selection compares 2 SoVITS checkpoints instead of ~5. Measured: _auto_save_every(22) == (22, 11).",
        "fix": "When the best count is far from SAVE_TARGET_COUNT (e.g. < 3), also try epochs+1 / epochs+2 (within the max) and pick the closest to 5 checkpoints. For 22 that gives 24 epochs, save every 4 (6 checkpoints).",
        "lens": "audio",
        "votes": "2/3",
        "confirmed": true,
        "evidence": "I tried to refute this and couldn't. The finding is accurate and you can reach it from the normal web UI, though it only bites in a narrow case.\n\nWhat I checked:\n- Ran `_auto_save_every` for epochs 1-50 in gsv39 with PYTHONPATH set to the repo.\n  - It returns (22,11), (26,13), (34,17), (38,19) and (46,23).\n  - Input 37 is bumped to 38 and also ends up at (38,19).\n  - Every one of these exports only 2 checkpoints.\n  - 9, 27, 33 and 39 give 3 checkpoints.\n- Cause: the loop at gptsovits.py:178-191 accepts any divisor that gives `count >= 2`. It only tries epochs+1 when the original number has no divisor at all, so 22 never gets compared with 24 (every 4, 6 checkpoints).\n- The function's own docstring says the target is \"3~8 个\" (3 to 8 checkpoints). Getting 2 contradicts both that docstring and R5b's \"SAVE MORE CHECKPOINTS\".\n\nCan a user actually hit it?\n- In the web UI the save interval is always auto. `_train_opts` passes only epochs, batch size and DPO, and there is no save_every widget.\n- The epoch fields (app.py:2642-2643) are free `gr.Number` inputs with minimum 0 and no step. Typing 22 for SoVITS, or 34/38/46 for GPT, triggers it.\n- GPT-SoVITS exports weights only at multiples of `save_every`. So selection (which tries up to SELECT_SOVITS=4 / SELECT_GPT=3) gets just e11 and e22 to choose from.\n\nWhy it's minor rather than major:\n- The default path is unaffected. Auto epochs are SoVITS 8 or 12 and GPT 15, which give 4, 6 and 5 checkpoints.\n- It only shows up when the teacher types one of these specific numbers by hand.\n\nWhy it still matters:\n- R5b explicitly asks for more checkpoints to choose from.\n- The cases are mostly high GPT epoch counts, where the code's own comment says overtraining is a risk (missing words, repetition; issue #2508). There, having only e23 and e46 to choose between is a real loss.\n- The previous release saved every 4 epochs, which would have given 5 checkpoints for 22 (though it missed the last epoch).\n\nThe fix is a few lines: also score epochs+1 and epochs+2, capped at the max, and keep the result closest to 5. For 22 that gives 24 with every 4 (6 checkpoints); for 46 it gives 48 with every 8 (6 checkpoints); both stay within the 25 and 50 limits.\n---\nI could not refute this. It is real, but it only happens on the manual-override path, so it stays minor.\n\nReproduced with the py3.9 venv (PYTHONPATH=repo), running `_auto_save_every(e)` for e = 1..50:\n- 22 gives (22, 11) and 26 gives (26, 13): 2 checkpoints each.\n- 34 gives (34, 17), 37 gives (38, 19), 38 gives (38, 19) and 46 gives (46, 23): also 2 checkpoints each.\n- Every other value from 3 to 50 gives 3 to 8 checkpoints.\n\nThe code is at voicetwin/backends/gptsovits.py:178-190. The divisor loop accepts `count >= 2`, and it tries epochs+1 only when no divisor qualifies at all. Since 22 = 2 x 11, count=2 (d=11) passes and epochs+1 is never tried. Upstream s2_train.py:514 exports only when `epoch % save_every_epoch == 0`, so only e11 and e22 exist. Selection wants SELECT_SOVITS=4 candidates, but it only gets 2.\n\nReasons it is not refuted:\n1. The function's own docstring says \"尽量让每个模型存 5 个左右（3~8 个…）\", but the code allows 2, so the code breaks its own stated contract.\n2. The web UI exposes only s_ep/g_ep/bs/dpo as gr.Number fields (app.py:2642-2643), not save_every. A teacher who types 22 in 音色训练轮数 always gets the auto interval, and the UI gives her no way to fix it (only config.yaml/CLI does).\n3. 22 is within SOVITS_MAX_EPOCHS=25, a plausible \"more is better\" choice. 26/34/37/38/46 are within GPT_MAX_EPOCHS=50.\n4. R5b explicitly asks to \"SAVE MORE CHECKPOINTS … so the automatic selection can choose the best epoch among more candidates\".\n\nLimits on severity:\n- The default auto path is unaffected. Epochs 8, 12 and 15 give 4, 6 and 5 checkpoints.\n- tests/test_gptsovits_fake.py:438 asserts `2 <= e // s <= 8`, so the author knowingly allowed 2. A fix will need that test bound updated (e.g. to `>= 3`).\n- The suggested fix is cheap and stays within the max: try epochs+1 and +2 when the best count is under 3. That gives 22 -> (24, 4) and 46 -> (48, 8).\n---\nThe arithmetic in the finding is correct, but the defect does not hold up as something that matters for this release.\n\nReproduced with gsv39 python, PYTHONPATH=repo: _auto_save_every gives 22->(22,11), 26->(26,13), 34->(34,17), 37->(38,19), 38->(38,19) and 46->(46,23). Each of these saves only 2 checkpoints. GPT-SoVITS really does export only at multiples of the save interval (ref s2_train.py:514, s1_train.py:50), so in these cases selection sees 2 candidates.\n\nWhy this is not a release-worthy defect:\n1. The automatic path that R5b is about never reaches this case. plan_training (voicetwin/backends/gptsovits.py:298-308) picks SoVITS 8 or 12 epochs and GPT 15 epochs. Those give (8,2)=4, (12,2)=6 and (15,3)=5 checkpoints, which is exactly the \"save more checkpoints\" behaviour R5b asks for. The same holds for every other auto-chosen epoch count.\n2. Hitting it takes a manual override in 高级设置: 音色训练轮数 (webui/app.py:2642, default 0 = 自动) set to exactly 22, or GPT set to 22/26/34/37/38/46. The target user is a non-technical teacher whom the requirements steer toward fully automatic settings, so this is a narrow edge case.\n3. Allowing 2 checkpoints is the intended design, enforced by a test. tests/test_gptsovits_fake.py:436 asserts `2 <= e // s <= 8 and e in (epochs, epochs + 1)` for every epoch count from 3 to 50. The function deliberately never moves the user's chosen epoch count by more than +1. The comment at line 191 confirms that an even count can always fall back to 2. The suggested fix changes the user's explicit 22 to 24, which contradicts that design and the respect for explicit overrides.\n4. Even in the edge case, selection still compares the final epoch and the midpoint (e11 and e22), and the plan summary says plainly \"每 11 轮存一次模型\". Nothing breaks and the final weights are not lost.\n\nThe only real gap is in the wording. The docstring says \"3~8 个\" and the default_config.yaml comment says \"5 个左右\", which does not match the 2-checkpoint result for these few manual inputs. That is cosmetic and minor, not a functional defect for v0.1.6."

Re-running the installer now really installs it, and the env-check and enhance hints say so. New `tests/test_installers.py`.\n2. **Variant switch leaves the download list stale** (two duplicate findings): `on_choose_variant` now returns `(player, files, note)` and the wiring outputs `out_files`. `choose_variant` also updates `overall_pct`.\n3. **\"高 X 个百分点\" when the recommended version has the lower %:** the gap is only shown when it is positive. Otherwise the line says the % is lower but the combined score is higher.\n4. **Answer card couldn't be graded later:** new \"📝 批改收上来的答题卡\" section in ⑤. You pick an earlier test (this also reloads its players), paste answers, and it calls `wf.grade_blind_test`. Answers are parsed by the new `wf.parse_blind_answers`. The card text now names that section.\n5. **1–2 uploaded originals deflate the %:** `from_files(..., project=)` now calibrates the voice's own clips against the uploaded centroid. If that isn't possible it uses the voice's own judge unchanged. The explanation line in the results now matches the reference actually used.\n6. **`similarity.target_pct` ignored:** it now works; `synth.tiers.perfect.target_pct` still takes priority.\n7. **Stale 去杂音 cache after redo** (two duplicate findings): `<key>.dn*.wav` is deleted when a new take is saved, and `_denoised` also rejects a cache file older than the wav.\n8. **Prepare ignores the filter:** prepare now takes `only_sus` and the table. The table refresh moved into a `.then` (`after_prepare_clips`), which also keeps edits typed before or during the run.\n9. **Retrain keeps the old model_id:** `_archive_old_run` also moves this voice's exported weights into `old_runs` and rewrites `models.json` paths, so the old selected model keeps working if a retrain is stopped. The run in use is never pruned. `model_id` now also hashes file size and mtime.\n10. **Unsaved table edits wiped by 采用建议 / filter / proofcheck** (two duplicate findings): those paths now take the current table and put unsaved edits back by id. Rows hidden by the filter stay at the bottom, and a \"N 条修改没有保存\" note appears. Proofcheck refreshes the table in a `.then`, so edits made during the run survive. \"🔄 重新载入\" still reloads from disk on purpose.\n11. **Answer key leaks to listeners:** the answer file now sits next to the folder as `盲听测试_<time>_答案（只给老师看，不要发给听众）.json`; old tests with `答案.json` inside still work. Submitting requires every item to be answered and then locks the choices. Unanswered items never show the truth.\n12. **Stale selection and speed after retrain:** `train()` drops `selection`, `speed` and `selection_error`, via a new `drop=` argument on `update_models`.\n13. **Real error not logged:** these paths now log with `exc_info=` (traceback goes to `voicetwin.log` and the console only; the page still shows Chinese) and store `repr(exc)` in `sources.json[\"error\"]` and `selection_error_detail`.\n14. **③ warning stale after prepare/train:** it now refreshes when the ③ tab opens and after prepare, train and select finish, and voice change uses the selected engine.\n15. **Clip table jumps:** the player, diff panel and adopt button moved below the table.\n16. **Phantom \"0\" row:** the result tables start with one blank row.\n17. **Plan preview drops keystrokes:** the epoch/batch boxes use `trigger_mode=\"always_last\"`, and Enter also refreshes the preview.\n18. **Stop confirm never expires:** a `.then` turns the button back to \"⏹ 停止\" after 5 s unless the stop was confirmed. A late second click now says \"确认超时了\".\n19. **ERes2NetV2 out of memory on long files:** audio is embedded in about 10 s windows and averaged, silent windows are skipped, and the CUDA cache is emptied afterwards.\n20. **Model download not kept awake:** new `wf.download_models` wraps the download in `keep_awake()`; the web UI uses it and the CLI download is wrapped too.\n21. **采用建议 with transcripts.csv open in Excel:** `set_clip_text` saves the change and returns `csv_locked`, the table still refreshes, and the page tells her to close Excel and save once more.\n22. **Chinese in strftime breaks on non-Chinese Windows:** new `_time_suffix()` builds the time text from `localtime` fields instead.\n23. **Double-click race at startup:** a single-instance file lock (fcntl, or msvcrt on Windows) makes a second window wait and then open the running one. On a port error it looks for the running instance before giving the (reworded) error.\n24. **DPO auto-enabled:** automatic mode never turns it on now; it is only used when chosen in 高级设置, config or the CLI. Help texts and tests are updated.\n25. **Perfect tier checks only the top candidate:** see item 7's neighbour above — it now stops as soon as any candidate meets the targets, selection prefers candidates that meet them, and the hint names the target that actually failed.\n26. **Too few checkpoints (e.g. 22 epochs → 2):** `_auto_save_every(epochs, max_epochs)` adds 1–2 epochs within the cap so at least 3 are saved, e.g. 22 → 24 epochs saving every 4.\n\nEach finding has a regression test except item 25's hint wording, which is covered inside the item 25 test. Item 14's tab and after-task wiring, items 15–17, and the variant/download wiring are checked through gradio 4.24 build/config tests."
